376	
377	When merging a PR:
378	
379	1. Find the task(s) it implements in the relevant week.
380	2. Flip `⬜` → `✅` (or `⬜` → `🟡` if partial).
381	3. Append the PR link: `— [PR #N](https://github.com/.../pull/N)`.
382	4. Update the per-week subtotal at the bottom of the week.
383	5. Update the **Snapshot** table at the top (calendar burn, task count).
384	6. If the PR added an out-of-roadmap task (e.g. ADR-driven scope change), add a row with the note.

Here's the current state of **DataQ**, as of today (2026-05-28):

## Headline
- **Week 1 of 8 — Foundation, ~80% complete.** Day 5 of 56 (~9% calendar burn).
- **Working tree clean, on `main`, no open PRs.** The Dependabot batch was cleared today.
- **6 roadmap tasks done + 2 in progress / 150 total (4%).**

## Week 1 exit gate
> *A logged-in user can hit a FastAPI endpoint that triggers GX against Snowflake DEV and persists a result row.*

**One thing stands between you and closing Week 1: PR 4** — GX Core wired to Snowflake DEV + result serialization to DB. That's the next milestone.

## What's landed
| Bundle | Status |
|---|---|
| PR 0 — governance (CLAUDE.md, ADRs 0001–0004/0009, CODEOWNERS, templates, Claude/Entire hooks) | ✅ |
| PR 1 — tooling lock (conda, Black, Ruff, mypy, pre-commit, CI, Dependabot) | ✅ |
| PR 2 a/b/c — Docker Compose, structlog + error envelope + FastAPI skeleton, SQLAlchemy models + Alembic baseline | ✅ |
| PR 3 a/b/c — Azure AD SSO end-to-end (backend MSAL + SecretStore abstraction + frontend MSAL + `/me`) | ✅ |

Recent merges also cleared the review-follow-up backlog: FK indexes (#70), CodeQL config (#69), type-check dep consolidation (#68), and the observability bundle — per-request logging + uvicorn bridge + PII/request-id hardening (#71).

## Still open in Week 1
- 🟡 Session timeout / silent token refresh — `InteractionRequiredAuthError` fallback pending (real-AAD smoke test deferred to Week 7)
- 🟡 Key Vault — abstraction landed, real Azure vault provisioning deferred to Week 7
- 🟡 Swagger/ReDoc — wired but "disable in prod" gate pending
- ⬜ **PR 4 (GX + Snowflake probe)** — the blocker for the exit gate

## Open issues (8)
No active blockers for current work. Notable:
- **#72** — `trigger_bindings` single-orchestrator-per-(provider,env) doc — *blocks Week 2 connection CRUD*
- **#65** — Vite 8 coordinated bump — Week 4
- **#62** — MSAL redirect lifecycle — Week 7
- #8, #10, #12, #17, #18, #19, #20 — deferred governance-era polish (slack work)

## Pending design decisions (must land before their week)
- **Severity tier weights** (ADR 0005) — before Week 3
- **ADF webhook auth** (ADR 0006) + **Airflow HMAC signing key** (ADR 0007) — before Week 2

**Bottom line:** Foundation is solid and clean — auth, models, observability, and tooling are all in. The single remaining task to close Week 1 is **PR 4**, the GX→Snowflake probe endpoint. After that, Week 2 (connection manager across all 4 datasources + orchestration webhooks) begins, but it's gated on the two pending ADRs and issue #72.

53	    quoted = ", ".join(f"'{v}'" for v in values)
54	    return CheckConstraint(f"{column} IN ({quoted})", name=name)

1	import re
2	import time
3	import uuid
4	from collections.abc import AsyncIterator, Awaitable, Callable
5	from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
6	from typing import Final
7	
8	from fastapi import FastAPI, Request, Response
9	
10	from backend.app.api.v1 import me as me_router
11	from backend.app.core.auth import init_auth
12	from backend.app.core.config import get_settings
13	from backend.app.core.errors import register_exception_handlers
14	from backend.app.core.logging import configure_logging, get_logger, request_id_var
15	
16	REQUEST_ID_HEADER: Final = "X-Request-ID"
17	# Validate caller-supplied X-Request-ID before echoing it (security audit
18	# 2026-05-28): cap length, restrict charset so log lines and response
19	# headers can't be polluted with arbitrary content.
20	_REQUEST_ID_RE: Final = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$")
21	
22	_log = get_logger(__name__)
23	
24	
25	@asynccontextmanager
26	async def lifespan(_app: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
27	    configure_logging()
28	    logger = get_logger(__name__)
29	    settings = get_settings()
30	    logger.info(
31	        "app_startup",
32	        environment=settings.environment,
33	        log_level=settings.log_level,
34	        app_insights_enabled=bool(settings.applicationinsights_connection_string),
35	    )
36	    await init_auth()
37	    yield
38	    logger.info("app_shutdown")
39	
40	
41	app = FastAPI(title="DataQ API", lifespan=lifespan)
42	
43	
44	@app.middleware("http")
45	async def request_id_middleware(
46	    request: Request, call_next: Callable[[Request], Awaitable[Response]]
47	) -> Response:
48	    incoming = request.headers.get(REQUEST_ID_HEADER)
49	    rid = incoming if incoming and _REQUEST_ID_RE.match(incoming) else uuid.uuid4().hex
50	    token = request_id_var.set(rid)
51	    start = time.perf_counter()
52	    try:
53	        response = await call_next(request)
54	    except Exception:
55	        elapsed_ms = round((time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000, 2)
56	        _log.exception(
57	            "request_failed",
58	            method=request.method,
59	            path=request.url.path,
60	            duration_ms=elapsed_ms,
61	        )
62	        request_id_var.reset(token)
63	        raise
64	    elapsed_ms = round((time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000, 2)
65	    _log.info(
66	        "request",
67	        method=request.method,
68	        path=request.url.path,
69	        status=response.status_code,
70	        duration_ms=elapsed_ms,
71	    )
72	    request_id_var.reset(token)
73	    response.headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = rid
74	    return response
75	
76	
77	register_exception_handlers(app)
78	
79	
80	app.include_router(me_router.router, prefix="/api/v1")
81	
82	
83	@app.get("/healthz")
84	async def healthz() -> dict[str, str]:
85	    return {"status": "ok"}
86

I have the full picture now. Here's the plan for **PR 4 — GX + Snowflake probe endpoint**, the slice that closes the Week 1 exit gate.

## Goal (the exit gate, verbatim)
> A logged-in user can hit a FastAPI endpoint that triggers GX against Snowflake DEV and persists a result row.

The CLAUDE.md smoke test names the target: `POST /api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite`. This is deliberately a **probe**, not the real connection/suite/check CRUD — that's Week 2/3. PR 4 proves the GX→Snowflake→DB thread end-to-end with the minimum scaffolding.

## What already exists (so I don't rebuild it)
- **Models**: `Connection`, `Suite`, `Check`, `Run`, `Result` all live in `db/models.py` — schema is ready, no migration needed.
- **GX pinned**: `great-expectations==1.17.2` in `environment.yml`.
- **SecretStore abstraction**: landed in PR 3b (`core/secrets.py`) — Snowflake creds resolve through it.
- **Auth**: `init_auth` + `/me` already wired; the probe just reuses the auth dependency.
- **Missing dirs**: `datasources/` and `services/` don't exist yet — PR 4 creates them.

## Proposed commits (one functionality each, per working agreements)

**1. `feat(datasources): Snowflake GX adapter`**
- New `backend/app/datasources/` package.
- A thin `SnowflakeDatasource` that builds a GX Core 1.17 Snowflake datasource + batch from a connection config dict, resolving credentials via the `SecretStore`.
- Keep the GX-version-specific API isolated here (CLAUDE.md warns GX v1 API drifts across point releases).

**2. `feat(services): GX run/result persistence`**
- New `backend/app/services/run_service.py`: takes a suite + its checks, runs the GX validation, maps the GX `ExpectationSuiteValidationResult` → `Run` (status, started/finished) + one `Result` row per check (`passed`/`failed`, `observed_value`, `expected_value`, `sample_failures`).
- **PII**: `sample_failures` flows through the logger redactor; persisted but governed by `sample_failures_retention_days` (already in config).

**3. `feat(api): _probe/snowflake-suite endpoint`**
- `POST /api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite`, auth-protected, full Swagger (`summary`, `tags`, `response_model`) — also ticks the partial W1 Swagger task.
- Seeds/looks up a dev Snowflake connection + a tiny canned suite (e.g. one `expect_table_row_count_to_be_between` / `expect_column_values_to_not_be_null`), runs it via the service, returns the persisted `run_id` + per-check results.

**4. `docs(progress): close Week 1 exit gate`**
- Flip the 3 GX tasks (lines 63–65 of `progress.md`) to ✅, bump the snapshot, update CLAUDE.md §13 to Week 2.

## Two decisions I'd want your call on before building

1. **Sync vs. Celery execution.** The async Celery runner is a Week 5 task. For the W1 probe I'd run GX **synchronously in-request** (simplest thing that satisfies the gate) and let Week 5 move it onto Celery. Alternative: wire Celery now to avoid rework.

2. **How the probe gets its connection.** No connection CRUD until Week 2. I'd **seed a single dev Snowflake connection** (config from env via the SecretStore) in the probe path, rather than build CRUD early. Alternative: read connection details straight from `Settings` and skip the `connections` row (but then nothing persists the link).

3. **Live Snowflake vs. mockable.** Do you have Snowflake DEV creds reachable from this machine for the manual test the working agreements require? If not, I'd structure the adapter so the GX call is injectable, and verify against a local fixture — flagging the real-Snowflake smoke as a deferred follow-up (same pattern as the deferred real-AAD test).

My default recommendation: **sync execution + seeded dev connection**, adapter built to be injectable so it's testable without live Snowflake. Want me to go ahead and draft this as a plan (EnterPlanMode) or start on commit 1 — and do you have Snowflake DEV credentials available?

> AGENT

Important finding: Celery is **pinned but not wired** — `celery[redis]==5.6.3` is in `environment.yml` and Redis is in compose, but there's no Celery app, no worker service in `docker-compose.yml`, and no tasks. (Compose currently only runs postgres + redis — the FastAPI/React/worker services aren't there yet either.) So "wire Celery now" expands PR 4 to stand up the whole async execution backbone. Here's the revised plan.

## Revised commit breakdown

**1. `feat(worker): Celery app + request_id propagation`**
- New `backend/app/worker/celery_app.py` — Celery app bound to `redis_url` from `Settings`.
- **request_id propagation FastAPI → Celery → GX** (this is an explicit CLAUDE.md observability rule, not optional): inject `request_id` into the task headers on dispatch, restore it into `request_id_var` via a `task_prerun`/`before_task_publish` signal so worker logs correlate with the originating request.
- Add a `worker` service to `docker-compose.yml` (same image/env as the API, command `celery -A ... worker`), depends_on redis + postgres.

**2. `feat(datasources): Snowflake GX adapter (injectable)`**
- `backend/app/datasources/snowflake.py` — builds the GX Core 1.17 Snowflake datasource/batch from a connection config, creds via `SecretStore`.
- GX call sits behind a small injectable seam (a runner callable/Protocol) so tests pass a fixture result instead of hitting Snowflake. Real-Snowflake smoke → **deferred follow-up issue** (same pattern as the deferred real-AAD test).

**3. `feat(services): GX run/result persistence`**
- `run_service.py`: maps GX `ExpectationSuiteValidationResult` → `Run` + per-check `Result` rows. Drives the run lifecycle through the statuses already in the model: `queued` (on dispatch) → `running` (task start) → `succeeded`/`failed`.
- `sample_failures` persisted under the existing `sample_failures_retention_days` governance; flows through the logger redactor.

**4. `feat(worker): run_suite Celery task`**
- The task that ties 2+3 together: loads suite+checks, runs the adapter, persists results, updates run status. This is the Week 5 "Celery + Redis background task runner" task landing early.

**5. `feat(api): _probe/snowflake-suite + run read-back`**
- `POST /api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite` — auth-protected, seeds a single dev Snowflake connection + a canned suite (e.g. one row-count + one not-null check), creates the `Run` as `queued`, dispatches the Celery task, returns `run_id` immediately.
- `GET /api/v1/_probe/runs/{run_id}` — minimal read-back so the async result is verifiable (also seeds the Week 5 poll-progress endpoint). Full Swagger on both.

**6. `docs(progress): close Week 1 exit gate`**
- Flip GX tasks (progress.md 63–65) to ✅, mark Week 5's "Celery background runner" 🟡-early, bump snapshot + CLAUDE.md §13 to Week 2.

## Scope note
Because the async path returns immediately, the manual-test verification (working-agreement requirement) is: `POST` → get `run_id` → `GET` the run → see a persisted `Result` row with `passed`/`failed`. The injectable adapter lets this pass against a local fixture without live Snowflake.

Two things worth your nod before I start:
- **Worker in compose now** pulls forward the "all services wired up" compose gap — fine to include, or keep PR 4 to just the Celery app + a documented `celery worker` command and leave the compose service for a separate chore?
- I'll open the **deferred real-Snowflake smoke** issue as part of this so it's tracked, not dropped.

1	import logging
2	import sys
3	from contextvars import ContextVar
4	from typing import Any
5	
6	import structlog
7	from structlog.types import EventDict, Processor
8	
9	from backend.app.core.config import get_settings
10	
11	request_id_var: ContextVar[str | None] = ContextVar("request_id", default=None)
12	
13	_PII_KEYS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
14	    {
15	        # Credentials
16	        "password",
17	        "passwd",
18	        "secret",
19	        "token",
20	        "api_key",
21	        "apikey",
22	        "authorization",
23	        "auth",
24	        "access_key",
25	        "private_key",
26	        # Personal contact
27	        "email",
28	        "phone",
29	        "ssn",
30	        "credit_card",
31	        "card_number",
32	        # Azure AD claims (per 2026-05-28 security audit) — AAD object IDs
33	        # and identifiers are GDPR-grade personal data under Article 4(1).
34	        "oid",
35	        "aad_oid",
36	        "aad_object_id",
37	        "upn",
38	        "preferred_username",
39	        "user_id",
40	        "name",
41	        "display_name",
42	    }
43	)
44	_REDACTED = "<redacted>"
45	
46	
47	def _redact_pii(_logger: Any, _name: str, event_dict: EventDict) -> EventDict:
48	    def walk(value: Any) -> Any:
49	        if isinstance(value, dict):
50	            return {k: (_REDACTED if k.lower() in _PII_KEYS else walk(v)) for k, v in value.items()}
51	        if isinstance(value, list):
52	            return [walk(v) for v in value]
53	        return value
54	
55	    result: EventDict = walk(event_dict)
56	    return result
57	
58	
59	def _add_request_id(_logger: Any, _name: str, event_dict: EventDict) -> EventDict:
60	    rid = request_id_var.get()
61	    if rid is not None:
62	        event_dict["request_id"] = rid
63	    return event_dict
64	
65	
66	def configure_logging() -> None:
67	    settings = get_settings()
68	    level = getattr(logging, settings.log_level)
69	
70	    shared_processors: list[Processor] = [
71	        structlog.contextvars.merge_contextvars,
72	        _add_request_id,
73	        structlog.processors.add_log_level,
74	        structlog.processors.TimeStamper(fmt="iso", utc=True),
75	        _redact_pii,
76	    ]
77	
78	    # Bridge stdlib logging (uvicorn.access, uvicorn.error, etc.) through the
79	    # same processor chain so every line out of the app is JSON with a
80	    # request_id when available. Without this the uvicorn access log emits
81	    # human-readable text that App Insights can't correlate (#50).
82	    formatter = structlog.stdlib.ProcessorFormatter(
83	        foreign_pre_chain=shared_processors,
84	        processors=[
85	            structlog.stdlib.ProcessorFormatter.remove_processors_meta,
86	            structlog.processors.dict_tracebacks,
87	            structlog.processors.JSONRenderer(),
88	        ],
89	    )
90	
91	    handler = logging.StreamHandler(sys.stdout)
92	    handler.setFormatter(formatter)
93	    root = logging.getLogger()
94	    root.handlers = [handler]
95	    root.setLevel(level)
96	
97	    # Detach uvicorn's pre-configured handlers; let logs propagate to root so
98	    # they hit the structlog ProcessorFormatter above.
99	    for name in ("uvicorn", "uvicorn.access", "uvicorn.error"):
100	        uvicorn_logger = logging.getLogger(name)
101	        uvicorn_logger.handlers = []
102	        uvicorn_logger.propagate = True
103	
104	    conn = settings.applicationinsights_connection_string
105	    if conn:
106	        from opencensus.ext.azure.log_exporter import AzureLogHandler
107	
108	        ai_handler = AzureLogHandler(connection_string=conn)
109	        ai_handler.setLevel(level)
110	        root.addHandler(ai_handler)
111	
112	    structlog.configure(
113	        processors=[
114	            *shared_processors,
115	            structlog.processors.dict_tracebacks,
116	            structlog.stdlib.ProcessorFormatter.wrap_for_formatter,
117	        ],
118	        wrapper_class=structlog.make_filtering_bound_logger(level),
119	        logger_factory=structlog.stdlib.LoggerFactory(),
120	        cache_logger_on_first_use=True,
121	    )
122	
123	
124	def get_logger(name: str | None = None) -> structlog.stdlib.BoundLogger:
125	    logger: structlog.stdlib.BoundLogger = structlog.get_logger(name)
126	    return logger
127	
```

> AGENT

Real blocker worth surfacing before I write the compose change: **there's no backend Dockerfile or backend/API service in compose at all.** Compose today is infra-only (postgres + redis); the API runs on the host via conda (`conda activate dataq` then `docker-compose up`). The CLAUDE.md quickstart *claims* compose runs FastAPI + worker, but that's aspirational — those services don't exist yet, and containerizing the backend is a Week 7 roadmap task.

So "add worker in compose" can't just append a service — there's no image for it to run. This is a genuine fork:

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/worker/celery_app.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Celery application for DataQ's async execution backbone.\n\nGX suite runs are dispatched from FastAPI and executed here so the request\nthread returns immediately (run created as ``queued``, worker drives it to\n``running`` → ``succeeded``/``failed``).\n\nThe CLAUDE.md observability rule requires ``request_id`` correlation to flow\nFastAPI → Celery → GX. We carry it on the task message headers: the caller\ninjects the active request_id on publish, and the worker restores it into the\nsame ``request_id_var`` ContextVar the structlog processor chain reads from, so\nworker log lines correlate with the request that triggered them.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom celery import Celery\nfrom celery.signals import (\n    before_task_publish,\n    setup_logging,\n    task_postrun,\n    task_prerun,\n)\n\nfrom backend.app.core.config import get_settings\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import configure_logging, get_logger, request_id_var\n\n# Message-header key carrying the originating request_id across the broker.\nREQUEST_ID_HEADER = \"request_id\"\n\n_log = get_logger(__name__)\n\n\ndef create_celery_app() -> Celery:\n    settings = get_settings()\n    app = Celery(\n        \"dataq\",\n        broker=settings.redis_url,\n        backend=settings.redis_url,\n    )\n    app.conf.update(\n        task_serializer=\"json\",\n        result_serializer=\"json\",\n        accept_content=[\"json\"],\n        timezone=\"UTC\",\n        enable_utc=True,\n        # Surface a 'started' state so the run-status read-back can distinguish\n        # queued from running without waiting for completion.\n        task_track_started=True,\n    )\n    return app\n\n\ncelery_app = create_celery_app()\n\n\n@setup_logging.connect\ndef _configure_celery_logging(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Disable Celery's default logging and route worker logs through structlog.\n\n    Connecting any receiver to ``setup_logging`` tells Celery not to configure\n    logging itself, so our JSON + PII-redacting processor chain stays in force\n    inside the worker exactly as it is in the API.\n    \"\"\"\n    configure_logging()\n\n\n@before_task_publish.connect\ndef _inject_request_id(headers: dict[str, Any] | None = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Caller side (FastAPI): stamp the active request_id onto task headers.\"\"\"\n    if headers is None:\n        return\n    rid = request_id_var.get()\n    if rid is not None:\n        headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = rid\n\n\n@task_prerun.connect\ndef _restore_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Worker side: restore request_id from the message into the ContextVar.\n\n    Custom headers added in ``before_task_publish`` are exposed as attributes on\n    ``task.request`` under the protocol-v2 message format.\n    \"\"\"\n    rid = getattr(task.request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, None) if task is not None else None\n    if rid:\n        request_id_var.set(rid)\n\n\n@task_postrun.connect\ndef _clear_request_id(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Worker side: clear the ContextVar so the next task starts uncorrelated.\"\"\"\n    request_id_var.set(None)\n"
}
```

> AGENT

`celery.*` is already in the `ignore_missing_imports` overrides, but celery ships `py.typed`, so mypy reads its real (unannotated) signal signatures — and `strict = true` enables `disallow_untyped_decorators`. The localized, honest fix is a targeted `# type: ignore[misc]` on each signal decorator. Applying to all four:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_0168FdaGSQKSqEmjcbg89yM1
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/worker/celery_app.py",
  "old_string": "@setup_logging.connect\ndef _configure_celery_logging(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:",
  "new_string": "@setup_logging.connect  # type: ignore[misc]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated\ndef _configure_celery_logging(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:"
}
```

1	"""Celery application for DataQ's async execution backbone.
2	
3	GX suite runs are dispatched from FastAPI and executed here so the request
4	thread returns immediately (run created as ``queued``, worker drives it to
5	``running`` → ``succeeded``/``failed``).
6	
7	The CLAUDE.md observability rule requires ``request_id`` correlation to flow
8	FastAPI → Celery → GX. We carry it on the task message headers: the caller
9	injects the active request_id on publish, and the worker restores it into the
10	same ``request_id_var`` ContextVar the structlog processor chain reads from, so
11	worker log lines correlate with the request that triggered them.
12	"""
13	
14	from typing import Any
15	
16	from celery import Celery
17	from celery.signals import (
18	    before_task_publish,
19	    setup_logging,
20	    task_postrun,
21	    task_prerun,
22	)
23	
24	from backend.app.core.config import get_settings
25	from backend.app.core.logging import configure_logging, request_id_var
26	
27	# Message-header key carrying the originating request_id across the broker.
28	REQUEST_ID_HEADER = "request_id"
29	
30	
31	def create_celery_app() -> Celery:
32	    settings = get_settings()
33	    app = Celery(
34	        "dataq",
35	        broker=settings.redis_url,
36	        backend=settings.redis_url,
37	    )
38	    app.conf.update(
39	        task_serializer="json",
40	        result_serializer="json",
41	        accept_content=["json"],
42	        timezone="UTC",
43	        enable_utc=True,
44	        # Surface a 'started' state so the run-status read-back can distinguish
45	        # queued from running without waiting for completion.
46	        task_track_started=True,
47	    )
48	    return app
49	
50	
51	celery_app = create_celery_app()
52	
53	
54	@setup_logging.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
55	def _configure_celery_logging(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:
56	    """Disable Celery's default logging and route worker logs through structlog.
57	
58	    Connecting any receiver to ``setup_logging`` tells Celery not to configure
59	    logging itself, so our JSON + PII-redacting processor chain stays in force
60	    inside the worker exactly as it is in the API.
61	    """
62	    configure_logging()
63	
64	
65	@before_task_publish.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
66	def _inject_request_id(headers: dict[str, Any] | None = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
67	    """Caller side (FastAPI): stamp the active request_id onto task headers."""
68	    if headers is None:
69	        return
70	    rid = request_id_var.get()
71	    if rid is not None:
72	        headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = rid
73	
74	
75	@task_prerun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
76	def _restore_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
77	    """Worker side: restore request_id from the message into the ContextVar.
78	
79	    Custom headers added in ``before_task_publish`` are exposed as attributes on
80	    ``task.request`` under the protocol-v2 message format.
81	    """
82	    rid = getattr(task.request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, None) if task is not None else None
83	    if rid:
84	        request_id_var.set(rid)
85	
86	
87	@task_postrun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
88	def _clear_request_id(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:
89	    """Worker side: clear the ContextVar so the next task starts uncorrelated."""
90	    request_id_var.set(None)
91	
```

Commit 1 is in. ✅

## Summary

**Branch:** `feature/pr4a-celery-app` · **Commit:** `843eacd`

**What landed — the async execution backbone:**
- `backend/app/worker/celery_app.py` — Celery app bound to `redis_url` (broker + result backend), JSON serialization, `task_track_started=True` so the upcoming run-status read-back can distinguish `queued` from `running`.
- **request_id propagation FastAPI → Celery → GX** (the CLAUDE.md observability rule): `before_task_publish` stamps the active request_id onto task headers → `task_prerun` restores it into the same `request_id_var` ContextVar structlog reads → `task_postrun` clears it.
- `setup_logging` hijacked so worker logs flow through the existing JSON + PII-redacting processor chain, not Celery's default.

**Verified:** Black / Ruff / mypy all green on the `dataq` env; smoke-tested both directions of request_id propagation (inject-on-publish reads the ContextVar; restore-on-prerun reads `task.request`). No tasks registered yet — the `run_suite` task is a later commit, so the true dispatch→worker-log correlation gets its end-to-end test then.

**One small decision I made:** kept this commit pure-Python (no Docker), splitting the **`backend/Dockerfile` + `api`/`worker` compose services** into the next commit — containerizing the backend is its own functionality and shouldn't ride in the Celery-app commit.

## Next up — Commit 2: containerize backend
Per your "worker + API both containerized" choice, that means:
- A `backend/Dockerfile` (conda-based, matching the locked `environment.yml`).
- `api` + `worker` services in `docker-compose.yml`, both building from it, alongside the existing postgres + redis.
- This effectively pulls the Week 7 "containerise FastAPI + Celery" task forward — I'll note that in `progress.md` when I update it at the end of the PR.

```
===setup.sh===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# One-command dev environment setup from a fresh clone.
# Usage: ./scripts/setup.sh
# Requires: conda (miniconda/miniforge), docker, pnpm, git

set -euo pipefail

CYAN='\033[0;36m'; GREEN='\033[0;32m'; RED='\033[0;31m'; NC='\033[0m'
step() { echo -e "${CYAN}▶ $1${NC}"; }
ok()   { echo -e "${GREEN}✓ $1${NC}"; }
die()  { echo -e "${RED}✗ $1${NC}" >&2; exit 1; }

# ── Prerequisites ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
step "Checking prerequisites"
command -v conda  >/dev/null || die "conda not found — install miniconda or miniforge first"
command -v docker >/dev/null || die "docker not found"
command -v pnpm   >/dev/null || die "pnpm not found — run: npm install -g pnpm"
command -v git    >/dev/null || die "git not found"
ok "Prerequisites OK"

# ── Conda environment ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
step "Creating / updating conda environment 'dataq'"
if conda env list | grep -q "^dataq "; then
  conda env update -n dataq -f environment.yml --prune
  ok "Conda env updated"
else
  conda env create -f environment.yml
  ok "Conda env created"
fi

# ── Pre-commit hooks ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
step "Installing pre-commit hooks"
conda run -n dataq pre-commit install --install-hooks
ok "Pre-commit hooks installed"

# ── Frontend dependencies ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
step "Installing frontend dependencies (pnpm)"
(cd frontend && pnpm install)
ok "Frontend dependencies installed"

# ── Docker services ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
step "Starting Docker services (Postgres, Redis)"
docker compose up -d postgres redis
ok "Docker services started"

# ── Database migrations ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
step "Waiting for Postgres to be ready"
for i in $(seq 1 30); do
  if docker compose exec -T postgres pg_isready -U dataq >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    ok "Postgres ready"
    break
  fi
  [ "$i" -eq 30 ] && die "Postgres did not become ready in time"
  sleep 1
done

step "Running Alembic migrations"
conda run -n dataq sh -c "cd backend && alembic upgrade head"
ok "Migrations applied"

# ── Seed data ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
step "Seeding dev data"
if [ -f backend/scripts/seed_dev.py ]; then
  conda run -n dataq python backend/scripts/seed_dev.py
  ok "Dev data seeded"
else
  echo "  (no seed script yet — skipping)"
fi

# ── Done ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
echo ""
echo -e "${GREEN}━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}  DataQ dev environment ready!${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━${NC}"
echo ""
echo "  Next steps:"
echo "    conda activate dataq"
echo "    docker compose up          # start all services"
echo "    # API: http://localhost:8000/docs"
echo "    # UI:  http://localhost:3000"
echo ""
===.env.example===
# Copy to .env and adjust. .env is gitignored.

ENVIRONMENT=dev
LOG_LEVEL=INFO

POSTGRES_USER=dataq
POSTGRES_PASSWORD=REDACTED
POSTGRES_DB=dataq
DATABASE_URL=REDACTED

REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0

# Optional: leave empty locally; set via Container Apps secret in production.
APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING=

# Days to retain results.sample_failures (potentially PII) before the W5 sweep nulls them.
SAMPLE_FAILURES_RETENTION_DAYS=30

# ── Azure AD SSO ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Leave empty + set AUTH_DEV_BYPASS=true for local dev without a real tenant.
# When populated, requests must carry a valid Azure AD bearer token.
AZURE_TENANT_ID=
AZURE_API_CLIENT_ID=
AZURE_SPA_CLIENT_ID=
AZURE_API_SCOPE=user_impersonation

# DEV ONLY. When true AND environment=dev AND Azure vars empty, auth is
# bypassed and requests resolve to a fixed dev user. Fails closed in any
# other environment regardless of this flag's value.
AUTH_DEV_BYPASS=true

# ── Secret store ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 'env' = read connection secrets from KV_SECRET_<NAME> env vars (local dev).
# 'azure_key_vault' = read from Azure Key Vault (staging / prod); requires
#                     AZURE_KEY_VAULT_URL. Auth via DefaultAzureCredential.
SECRET_STORE=env
AZURE_KEY_VAULT_URL=

# Example connection secret (local dev only). Pattern: KV_SECRET_<UPPER_SNAKE_NAME>.
# Matches Connection.secret_ref='snowflake-uat-finance' to this env var.
# KV_SECRET_SNOWFLAKE_UAT_FINANCE=changeme

# ── Frontend (Vite) auth mirrors ──────────────────────────────────────────────
# Vite only exposes env vars prefixed VITE_ to client code. These mirror the
# backend AZURE_* vars and AUTH_DEV_BYPASS so the SPA and the API agree on the
# tenant + client IDs and bypass behaviour.
VITE_AZURE_TENANT_ID=
VITE_AZURE_SPA_CLIENT_ID=
VITE_AZURE_API_CLIENT_ID=
VITE_AZURE_API_SCOPE=user_impersonation
VITE_AUTH_DEV_BYPASS=true

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/Dockerfile",
  "content": "# DataQ backend image — serves both the FastAPI API and the Celery worker.\n# Build context is the repo ROOT (not backend/) because the app imports as the\n# top-level `backend.app.*` package and environment.yml lives at the root:\n#   docker build -f backend/Dockerfile .\n#\n# Conda-only per CLAUDE.md (no venv/poetry). The dataq env is created from the\n# pinned environment.yml and its bin dir is put first on PATH so `uvicorn`,\n# `celery`, `alembic`, and `python` resolve to it without a `conda run` wrapper\n# (which would interfere with signal forwarding to uvicorn/celery).\nFROM continuumio/miniconda3:24.11.1-0\n\nWORKDIR /workspace\n\n# Create the conda env first and cache the layer — only re-runs when the pinned\n# environment.yml changes, not on every code edit.\nCOPY environment.yml ./\nRUN conda env create -f environment.yml && conda clean -afy\n\nENV PATH=/opt/conda/envs/dataq/bin:$PATH \\\n    PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1\n\n# Application code + tooling config (pyproject is needed to run pytest/mypy in-container).\nCOPY pyproject.toml ./\nCOPY backend ./backend\n\nEXPOSE 8000\n\n# Default to the API; the worker service overrides `command` in compose.\nCMD [\"uvicorn\", \"backend.app.main:app\", \"--host\", \"0.0.0.0\", \"--port\", \"8000\"]\n"
}

While it builds, here's where commit 2 stands:

**Files created/changed:**
- `backend/Dockerfile` — conda-based (`continuumio/miniconda3`), creates the pinned `dataq` env, puts its bin dir on `PATH` so `uvicorn`/`celery`/`alembic` resolve without a `conda run` wrapper. Default CMD runs the API; worker overrides `command`.
- `.dockerignore` — keeps the root build context lean (excludes `frontend/`, `.git`, caches, `.env`).
- `docker-compose.yml` — added `api` (port 8000, `--reload`, source bind-mount) and `worker` (`celery -A backend.app.worker.celery_app worker`) services, both building from `backend/Dockerfile` with `DATABASE_URL`/`REDIS_URL` pointed at the `postgres`/`redis` service hostnames, gated on their healthchecks.

**Two corrections vs. the original preview I want to flag:**
- Build **context is the repo root** with `dockerfile: backend/Dockerfile` (not `build: ./backend`) — required because the app imports as the top-level `backend.app.*` package and `environment.yml` lives at the root.
- `.env` is referenced as **optional** (`required: false`) so `docker compose` works on a fresh clone before anyone copies `.env.example`.

## Summary

**Branch:** `feature/pr4a-celery-app` · **Commit:** `ef14d0b`

**What landed — containerized backend:**
- `backend/Dockerfile` — conda-based (`continuumio/miniconda3:24.11.1-0`), creates the pinned `dataq` env from `environment.yml` as a cached layer, puts the env's bin dir on `PATH` so `uvicorn`/`celery`/`alembic` run directly (no `conda run` wrapper that would break signal forwarding).
- `.dockerignore` — keeps the root build context lean (excludes `frontend/`, `.git`, caches, `.env`).
- `docker-compose.yml` — `api` (port 8000, `--reload`, source bind-mount) + `worker` (`celery -A backend.app.worker.celery_app worker`), both building from the Dockerfile, with `DATABASE_URL`/`REDIS_URL` pointed at the `postgres`/`redis` service hostnames and gated on their healthchecks.

**Verified end-to-end (the manual test):**
- Image builds clean (exit 0).
- App + Celery import inside the container with the env resolved via `PATH`.
- Worker boots, **connects to the broker** at `redis://redis:6379/0`, and emits **structlog JSON** — confirming commit 1's `setup_logging` hijack holds in the worker process.
- `docker compose config` validates.

**Scope note:** this pulls the Week 7 "containerise FastAPI + Celery" roadmap task forward — I'll record that in `progress.md` at PR close.

## Where we are in PR 4
- ✅ Commit 1 — Celery app + request_id propagation
- ✅ Commit 2 — Dockerfile + api/worker compose services
- ⬜ Commit 3 — Snowflake GX adapter (injectable)
- ⬜ Commit 4 — GX run/result persistence service
- ⬜ Commit 5 — `run_suite` Celery task
- ⬜ Commit 6 — `_probe/snowflake-suite` endpoint + run read-back
- ⬜ Commit 7 — docs/progress + CLAUDE.md §13

Next is **commit 3, the Snowflake GX adapter** — built behind an injectable seam so it's testable without live Snowflake (real-Snowflake smoke deferred to a tracked follow-up issue). Want me to proceed?

> DEVELOPER

are we bundelling everything in a single PR or splitting it like PR 3?

> AGENT

Good question — and it matters because of a working-agreement detail: **`main` is squash-merge only.** That means every PR collapses into exactly **one** commit on `main`, regardless of how many commits are on the branch. So the granular "one functionality per commit" history I've been building (Celery app vs. containerization vs. adapter…) only survives on `main` if those functionalities land as **separate PRs**. That's exactly why PR 3 was split into 3a/3b/3c — three squash commits, three `/review` passes, three short-lived branches.

So to stay consistent with PR 3 and the trunk-based / short-lived-branch agreement, I'd lean toward splitting. Here are the natural seams:

Stands up the **async execution backbone** the probe's GX runs will dispatch onto — split out as **PR 4a** (mirrors PR 3a/b/c) since `main` is squash-merge only and this is independently valuable infrastructure with no dependency on the GX work.\n\n- **Celery app + request_id propagation.** New `backend/app/worker/celery_app.py` bound to `redis_url` (broker + result backend), JSON serialization, `task_track_started=True`. restore-on-prerun reads `task.request`.\n\n## Security / risk notes\n\n- No public endpoints added; no new secrets. `.env` referenced as `required: false` and excluded via `.dockerignore` so nothing is baked into the image.\n- Worker logs go through the existing PII-redacting structlog processor chain (important once GX sample-failure rows flow through in 4c).\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..19c3689
--- /dev/null
+++ b/.dockerignore
@@ -0,0 +1,31 @@
+# Build context is the repo root; keep it small and deterministic.
+.git
+.github
+.entire
+.claude
+
+# Frontend has its own (future) image — never ship its deps into the backend.
+frontend
+
+# Python / tooling caches
+__pycache__
+**/__pycache__
+*.pyc
+.mypy_cache
+.pytest_cache
+.ruff_cache
+.coverage
+htmlcov
+
+# Local env / secrets — never bake into an image
+.env
+*.env
+!.env.example
+
+# Docs / context not needed at runtime
+docs
+context
+*.md
+
+# OS cruft
+.DS_Store
diff --git a/backend/Dockerfile b/backend/Dockerfile
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..6a510c2
--- /dev/null
+++ b/backend/Dockerfile
@@ -0,0 +1,29 @@
+# DataQ backend image — serves both the FastAPI API and the Celery worker.
+# Build context is the repo ROOT (not backend/) because the app imports as the
+# top-level `backend.app.*` package and environment.yml lives at the root:
+#   docker build -f backend/Dockerfile .
+#
+# Conda-only per CLAUDE.md (no venv/poetry). The dataq env is created from the
+# pinned environment.yml and its bin dir is put first on PATH so `uvicorn`,
+# `celery`, `alembic`, and `python` resolve to it without a `conda run` wrapper
+# (which would interfere with signal forwarding to uvicorn/celery).
+FROM continuumio/miniconda3:24.11.1-0
+
+WORKDIR /workspace
+
+# Create the conda env first and cache the layer — only re-runs when the pinned
+# environment.yml changes, not on every code edit.
+COPY environment.yml ./
+RUN conda env create -f environment.yml && conda clean -afy
+
+ENV PATH=/opt/conda/envs/dataq/bin:$PATH \
+    PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1
+
+# Application code + tooling config (pyproject is needed to run pytest/mypy in-container).
+COPY pyproject.toml ./
+COPY backend ./backend
+
+EXPOSE 8000
+
+# Default to the API; the worker service overrides `command` in compose.
+CMD ["uvicorn", "backend.app.main:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]
diff --git a/backend/app/worker/__init__.py b/backend/app/worker/__init__.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..e69de29
diff --git a/backend/app/worker/celery_app.py b/backend/app/worker/celery_app.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..ab85996
--- /dev/null
+++ b/backend/app/worker/celery_app.py
@@ -0,0 +1,90 @@
+"""Celery application for DataQ's async execution backbone.
+
+GX suite runs are dispatched from FastAPI and executed here so the request
+thread returns immediately (run created as ``queued``, worker drives it to
+``running`` → ``succeeded``/``failed``).
+
+The CLAUDE.md observability rule requires ``request_id`` correlation to flow
+FastAPI → Celery → GX. We carry it on the task message headers: the caller
+injects the active request_id on publish, and the worker restores it into the
+same ``request_id_var`` ContextVar the structlog processor chain reads from, so
+worker log lines correlate with the request that triggered them.
+"""
+
+from typing import Any
+
+from celery import Celery
+from celery.signals import (
+    before_task_publish,
+    setup_logging,
+    task_postrun,
+    task_prerun,
+)
+
+from backend.app.core.config import get_settings
+from backend.app.core.logging import configure_logging, request_id_var
+
+# Message-header key carrying the originating request_id across the broker.
+REQUEST_ID_HEADER = "request_id"
+
+
+def create_celery_app() -> Celery:
+    settings = get_settings()
+    app = Celery(
+        "dataq",
+        broker=settings.redis_url,
+        backend=settings.redis_url,
+    )
+    app.conf.update(
+        task_serializer="json",
+        result_serializer="json",
+        accept_content=["json"],
+        timezone="UTC",
+        enable_utc=True,
+        # Surface a 'started' state so the run-status read-back can distinguish
+        # queued from running without waiting for completion.
+        task_track_started=True,
+    )
+    return app
+
+
+celery_app = create_celery_app()
+
+
+@setup_logging.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
+def _configure_celery_logging(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:
+    """Disable Celery's default logging and route worker logs through structlog.
+
+    Connecting any receiver to ``setup_logging`` tells Celery not to configure
+    logging itself, so our JSON + PII-redacting processor chain stays in force
+    inside the worker exactly as it is in the API.
+    """
+    configure_logging()
+
+
+@before_task_publish.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
+def _inject_request_id(headers: dict[str, Any] | None = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
+    """Caller side (FastAPI): stamp the active request_id onto task headers."""
+    if headers is None:
+        return
+    rid = request_id_var.get()
+    if rid is not None:
+        headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = rid
+
+
+@task_prerun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
+def _restore_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
+    """Worker side: restore request_id from the message into the ContextVar.
+
+    Custom headers added in ``before_task_publish`` are exposed as attributes on
+    ``task.request`` under the protocol-v2 message format.
+    """
+    rid = getattr(task.request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, None) if task is not None else None
+    if rid:
+        request_id_var.set(rid)
+
+
+@task_postrun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
+def _clear_request_id(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:
+    """Worker side: clear the ContextVar so the next task starts uncorrelated."""
+    request_id_var.set(None)
diff --git a/docker-compose.yml b/docker-compose.yml
index b02928b..d9bbf1d 100644
--- a/docker-compose.yml
+++ b/docker-compose.yml
@@ -25,5 +25,49 @@ services:
       timeout: 3s
       retries: 10
 
+  # FastAPI + Celery share one image (backend/Dockerfile, conda env `dataq`).
+  # DATABASE_URL / REDIS_URL are overridden here to the in-network service
+  # hostnames (the .env defaults point at localhost for host-side dev).
+  api:
+    build:
+      context: .
+      dockerfile: backend/Dockerfile
+    command: uvicorn backend.app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --reload
+    ports:
+      - "8000:8000"
+    env_file:
+      - path: .env
+        required: false
+    environment:
+      DATABASE_URL: REDACTED
+      REDIS_URL: redis://redis:6379/0
+    volumes:
+      # Bind-mount source for hot reload; the conda env in the image is untouched.
+      - ./backend:/workspace/backend
+    depends_on:
+      postgres:
+        condition: service_healthy
+      redis:
+        condition: service_healthy
+
+  worker:
+    build:
+      context: .
+      dockerfile: backend/Dockerfile
+    command: celery -A backend.app.worker.celery_app worker --loglevel=INFO
+    env_file:
+      - path: .env
+        required: false
+    environment:
+      DATABASE_URL: REDACTED
+      REDIS_URL: redis://redis:6379/0
+    volumes:
+      - ./backend:/workspace/backend
+    depends_on:
+      postgres:
+        condition: service_healthy
+      redis:
+        condition: service_healthy
+
 volumes:
   postgres_data:

```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ\ngh api repos/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pulls/74/comments -X POST -f commit_id=ef14d0b0b2285c08f2c3fdad272b34ace743494b -f path=backend/app/worker/celery_app.py -F line=90 -f side=RIGHT -f body='**[correctness — medium] request_id_var is cleared/set without token save+restore; clobbers the caller context under eager mode.**\n\n`_restore_request_id` does a bare `request_id_var.set(rid)` and `_clear_request_id` does `request_id_var.set(None)`. With the default prefork pool these signals fire in the *worker* process, so this is harmless today. But under `task_always_eager` (the usual setup for the Week 8 API/service tests, and likely the PR 4c tests that dispatch `run_suite`), `task_prerun`/`task_postrun` fire **inside the calling request'\\''s context** — so `postrun` resets `request_id_var` to `None` and drops the request_id for the *remainder of the request handler*, silently breaking the very correlation this module exists to provide.\n\nFix: capture the token and restore it, instead of blanket set/None — e.g. stash `token = request_id_var.set(rid)` on `task.request` in prerun and `request_id_var.reset(token)` in postrun (this mirrors how `request_id_middleware` in `main.py` already uses `reset(token)`). Worth fixing now while the handler is being written, since it will only surface as a confusing missing-request_id once eager-mode tests land.' 2>&1 | tail -3\necho \"=== comment 1 posted ===\"",
  "description": "Post finding 1 as inline comment"
}

> AGENT

Review complete. Both findings posted as inline comments on PR #74.

## `/code-review` summary — PR 4a (#74)

I reviewed the diff directly (5 files, +194 — too small to justify spinning up the 7-agent fan-out) across the correctness, removed-behavior, cross-file, and cleanup/altitude angles. Two findings survived verification; both are now inline comments (not silent fixes, per the working agreement):

| # | Severity | File | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **medium** | `celery_app.py:90` | `request_id_var` set/cleared with bare `set()`/`set(None)` instead of token save+restore. Harmless under prefork (signals fire in the worker process), but under `task_always_eager` (the normal test setup) `task_postrun` fires *in the caller's context* and nulls the request's `request_id` for the rest of the handler — silently breaking the correlation this module exists to provide. |
| 2 | low/med | `celery_app.py:82` | request_id propagation is unverified end-to-end; if the custom header doesn't surface on `task.request`, correlation silently no-ops (no error). Flagged for PR 4c to add an integration assertion when the `run_suite` task lands. |

Nothing else flagged — Dockerfile, `.dockerignore`, and compose checked out (env-on-PATH, build context, hostname overrides, healthcheck gating all correct). The container-runs-as-root hardening gap is real but legitimately Week-7 deploy scope, so I didn't post it.

## Recommendation
**Finding #1 is worth fixing in this PR before merge** — it's small, low-risk, and the correct pattern already exists in `main.py` (`request_id_middleware` uses `reset(token)`). I'd:
- stash `token = request_id_var.set(rid)` on `task.request` in `_restore_request_id`,
- `request_id_var.reset(token)` in `_clear_request_id` (guarding the no-token case),

then re-run the gates + worker smoke and push. **Finding #2** stays as a tracked note for 4c (it needs a real dispatched task to test).

Want me to apply the finding-#1 fix to PR 4a now, then move on to PR 4b?

  "old_string": "@task_prerun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated\ndef _restore_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Worker side: restore request_id from the message into the ContextVar.\n\n    Custom headers added in ``before_task_publish`` are exposed as attributes on\n    ``task.request`` under the protocol-v2 message format.\n    \"\"\"\n    rid = getattr(task.request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, None) if task is not None else None\n    if rid:\n        request_id_var.set(rid)\n\n\n@task_postrun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated\ndef _clear_request_id(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Worker side: clear the ContextVar so the next task starts uncorrelated.\"\"\"\n    request_id_var.set(None)",
  "new_string": "@task_prerun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated\ndef _restore_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Worker side: restore request_id from the message into the ContextVar.\n\n    Custom headers added in ``before_task_publish`` are exposed as attributes on\n    ``task.request`` under the protocol-v2 message format. We stash the reset\n    token on ``task.request`` so ``task_postrun`` can restore the *prior* value\n    rather than blindly clearing — under ``task_always_eager`` these signals run\n    in the caller's context, so a blanket reset would drop the request_id for\n    the rest of the request handler.\n    \"\"\"\n    rid = getattr(task.request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, None) if task is not None else None\n    if rid and task is not None:\n        token = request_id_var.set(rid)\n        setattr(task.request, _REQUEST_ID_TOKEN_ATTR, token)\n\n\n@task_postrun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated\ndef _clear_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Worker side: restore the ContextVar to its pre-task value.\n\n    Only resets when ``task_prerun`` actually set it (token present), mirroring\n    the ``reset(token)`` pattern used by ``request_id_middleware`` in main.py.\n    \"\"\"\n    token = getattr(task.request, _REQUEST_ID_TOKEN_ATTR, None) if task is not None else None\n    if token is not None:\n        request_id_var.reset(token)"
}

=== secrets.py ===
"""Secret resolution abstraction.

Two backends are supported, picked from `settings.secret_store`:

- **EnvSecretStore** — reads secrets from env vars prefixed `KV_SECRET_`.
  Local dev only — convenient when running against `docker-compose` without
  an Azure tenant. Name normalisation: `snowflake-uat-finance` →
  env var `KV_SECRET_SNOWFLAKE_UAT_FINANCE`.

- **AzureKeyVaultStore** — reads from Azure Key Vault via
  `azure-identity` (DefaultAzureCredential) + `azure-keyvault-secrets`.
  Production / staging. Real vault provisioning + tenant config land in
  Week 7 (deployment hardening); the code path is wired now so callers
  can take a dependency on `SecretStore` without waiting.

The Azure SDK is **lazy-imported** so EnvSecretStore-only deployments
don't pay the import cost.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import threading
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Final, Protocol, runtime_checkable

from backend.app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from azure.keyvault.secrets import SecretClient

log = get_logger(__name__)

ENV_PREFIX: Final = "KV_SECRET_"
_AKV_MODE: Final = "azure_key_vault"


class SecretNotFoundError(Exception):
    """Raised when the requested secret is missing or unreadable."""


@runtime_checkable
class SecretStore(Protocol):
    def get(self, name: str) -> str: ...


def _env_key(name: str) -> str:
    return f"{ENV_PREFIX}{name.upper().replace('-', '_')}"


class EnvSecretStore:
    """Resolves secrets from `KV_SECRET_*` env vars. Local dev only."""

    def get(self, name: str) -> str:
        key = _env_key(name)
        value = os.environ.get(key)
        if value is None:
            raise SecretNotFoundError(f"Env secret {key!r} not set (mapped from name={name!r})")
        return value


class AzureKeyVaultStore:
    """Resolves secrets from Azure Key Vault via DefaultAzureCredential."""

    def __init__(self, vault_url: str) -> None:
        self._vault_url = vault_url
        self._client: SecretClient | None = None
        self._lock = threading.Lock()

    def _client_lazy(self) -> SecretClient:
        if self._client is not None:
            return self._client
        with self._lock:
            if self._client is None:
                from azure.identity import DefaultAzureCredential
                from azure.keyvault.secrets import SecretClient

                self._client = SecretClient(
                    vault_url=self._vault_url,
                    credential=DefaultAzureCredential(),
                )
            return self._client

    def get(self, name: str) -> str:
        try:
            secret = self._client_lazy().get_secret(name)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise SecretNotFoundError(
                f"Key Vault secret {name!r} at {self._vault_url}: {exc}"
            ) from exc
        value = secret.value
        if value is None:
            raise SecretNotFoundError(f"Key Vault secret {name!r} has no value")
        return str(value)


_store_singleton: SecretStore | None = None
_store_lock = threading.Lock()


def _build_store(settings: Settings) -> SecretStore:
    if settings.secret_store == _AKV_MODE:
        if not settings.azure_key_vault_url:
            raise RuntimeError(f"secret_store={_AKV_MODE!r} requires AZURE_KEY_VAULT_URL")
        return AzureKeyVaultStore(settings.azure_key_vault_url)
    return EnvSecretStore()


def get_secret_store() -> SecretStore:
    """Return the configured store (cached after first call)."""
    global _store_singleton
    if _store_singleton is not None:
        return _store_singleton
    with _store_lock:
        if _store_singleton is None:
            settings = get_settings()
            _store_singleton = _build_store(settings)
            log.info("secret_store_initialized", backend=settings.secret_store)
        return _store_singleton


def reset_secret_store_cache() -> None:
    """Test-only: clear the cached store so the next call rebuilds it."""
    global _store_singleton
    with _store_lock:
        _store_singleton = None
```

> AGENT

Full GX 1.17.2 chain confirmed:
`add_snowflake(name, connection_string)` → `add_table_asset(name, table_name, schema_name)` → `add_batch_definition_whole_table(name)` → `ExpectationSuite(name, expectations=[...])` → `ValidationDefinition(name, data, suite).run(result_format=...)`. Asset-build connects to Snowflake, so the seam wraps the whole chain.

Here's the 4b design (consistent with your "injectable, fixture-verified" decision):
- `datasources/base.py` — the seam: a `CheckRunner` **Protocol** + GX-agnostic DTOs (`CheckSpec` in, `CheckOutcome`/`SuiteOutcome` out). This keeps GX fully isolated in the adapter (CLAUDE.md rule) and lets 4c's run-service + tests depend on the Protocol, not GX.
- `datasources/snowflake.py` — `SnowflakeConfig` (Pydantic), connection-string builder, and `SnowflakeCheckRunner` implementing the Protocol via GX, plus a factory that builds one from a `Connection` row + `SecretStore`.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/datasources/base.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Datasource adapter seam.\n\nEvery datasource (Snowflake now; ADLS / S3 / Unity Catalog later) executes DQ\nchecks behind one ``CheckRunner`` interface that speaks GX-agnostic DTOs. The\nGX-specific machinery lives entirely inside each adapter, so the run-service and\nits tests depend only on the types here — never on Great Expectations internals.\nThis is also the seam that lets v1.1 swap GX for DQX on Unity Catalog (CLAUDE.md\n§5) without rippling into the suite / check / result layer.\n\n`CheckSpec` goes in (a check pulled from the DB); `CheckOutcome` comes out, one\nper check, shaped to map cleanly onto the `results` table columns. Adapters\ntranslate GX results into these DTOs; tests provide a fake `CheckRunner` and\nnever touch a live datasource.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typing import Any, Protocol, runtime_checkable\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass CheckSpec:\n    \"\"\"One expectation to evaluate, sourced from a `checks` row.\n\n    `expectation_type` is the GX snake_case name (e.g.\n    ``expect_column_values_to_not_be_null``); `kwargs` are its parameters\n    (e.g. ``{\"column\": \"id\"}``). Adapters own the translation to the concrete\n    GX expectation class.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    expectation_type: str\n    kwargs: dict[str, Any]\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass CheckOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Result of one check, shaped for the `results` table.\n\n    `observed_value` / `expected_value` / `sample_failures` land in the\n    matching JSONB columns. `sample_failures` may contain real data rows, so it\n    is governed by the retention sweep and must only ever be logged through the\n    PII-redacting structlog chain.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    expectation_type: str\n    success: bool\n    observed_value: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n    expected_value: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n    sample_failures: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass SuiteOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Aggregate result of running a list of checks against one table.\"\"\"\n\n    success: bool\n    checks: list[CheckOutcome]\n\n\n@runtime_checkable\nclass CheckRunner(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Executes a set of checks against a single table and returns outcomes.\"\"\"\n\n    def run_checks(\n        self,\n        *,\n        table: str,\n        schema: str | None,\n        checks: list[CheckSpec],\n    ) -> SuiteOutcome: ...\n"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/datasources/snowflake.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Snowflake datasource adapter (GX Core 1.17).\n\nAll Great-Expectations-specific machinery for Snowflake lives here, behind the\n`CheckRunner` seam in ``base.py`` — per CLAUDE.md, the GX version-specific API\nmust not leak into the suite / check / result layer (GX v1 has drifted across\npoint releases).\n\nThe full GX chain (``add_snowflake`` → ``add_table_asset`` →\n``add_batch_definition_whole_table`` → ``ValidationDefinition.run``) connects to\nSnowflake at asset-build time, so ``run_checks`` cannot run without a live\nwarehouse. Tests therefore exercise the GX-free parts directly — config\nvalidation, connection-string building, the snake_case→GX-class translation,\nand the GX-result→`CheckOutcome` mapping (fed a canned result) — and inject a\nfake `CheckRunner` elsewhere. End-to-end validation against a real Snowflake DEV\nwarehouse is a tracked follow-up.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import Any\nfrom urllib.parse import quote_plus\n\nimport great_expectations as gx\nimport great_expectations.expectations as gxe\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field\n\nfrom backend.app.core.secrets import SecretStore\nfrom backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome\n\n# GX result keys that describe failing rows — copied into CheckOutcome.sample_failures.\n# These may contain real data, so they only ever reach logs via the redactor.\n_SAMPLE_KEYS = (\"partial_unexpected_list\", \"unexpected_count\", \"unexpected_percent\")\n\n\nclass UnknownExpectationError(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a check's expectation_type has no matching GX expectation.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass SnowflakeConfig(BaseModel):\n    \"\"\"Non-secret Snowflake connection config (the password comes from secrets).\n\n    Maps from ``Connection.config``. ``schema`` is aliased to ``schema_`` to\n    avoid shadowing pydantic's ``BaseModel.schema``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(populate_by_name=True, extra=\"forbid\")\n\n    account: str\n    user: str\n    database: str\n    schema_: str = Field(alias=\"schema\")\n    warehouse: str\n    role: str | None = None\n\n\ndef build_connection_string(config: SnowflakeConfig, password: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Assemble a snowflake-sqlalchemy URL. User/password/params are URL-encoded.\"\"\"\n    params = {\"warehouse\": config.warehouse}\n    if config.role:\n        params[\"role\"] = config.role\n    query = \"&\".join(f\"{key}={quote_plus(value)}\" for key, value in params.items())\n    return (\n        f\"snowflake://{quote_plus(config.user)}:{quote_plus(password)}\"\n        f\"@{config.account}/{config.database}/{config.schema_}?{query}\"\n    )\n\n\ndef _expectation_class_name(expectation_type: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"snake_case GX type → PascalCase class name.\n\n    ``expect_column_values_to_not_be_null`` → ``ExpectColumnValuesToNotBeNull``.\n    \"\"\"\n    return \"\".join(part.title() for part in expectation_type.split(\"_\"))\n\n\ndef _to_gx_expectation(spec: CheckSpec) -> Any:\n    class_name = _expectation_class_name(spec.expectation_type)\n    expectation_cls = getattr(gxe, class_name, None)\n    if expectation_cls is None:\n        raise UnknownExpectationError(\n            f\"Unknown expectation_type {spec.expectation_type!r} (no gx class {class_name!r})\"\n        )\n    return expectation_cls(**spec.kwargs)\n\n\ndef _extract_sample_failures(result: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    sample = {key: result[key] for key in _SAMPLE_KEYS if key in result}\n    return sample or None\n\n\ndef to_suite_outcome(gx_result: Any) -> SuiteOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Map a GX ExpectationSuiteValidationResult onto our GX-agnostic DTO.\n\n    Kept module-level (not a private method) so it is unit-testable with a\n    constructed GX result, no Snowflake connection required.\n    \"\"\"\n    outcomes: list[CheckOutcome] = []\n    for check_result in gx_result.results:\n        config = check_result.expectation_config\n        detail: dict[str, Any] = check_result.result or {}\n        observed = (\n            {\"observed_value\": detail[\"observed_value\"]} if \"observed_value\" in detail else None\n        )\n        outcomes.append(\n            CheckOutcome(\n                expectation_type=config.type,\n                success=bool(check_result.success),\n                observed_value=observed,\n                expected_value=dict(config.kwargs) if config.kwargs else None,\n                sample_failures=_extract_sample_failures(detail),\n            )\n        )\n    return SuiteOutcome(success=bool(gx_result.success), checks=outcomes)\n\n\nclass SnowflakeCheckRunner:\n    \"\"\"`CheckRunner` for Snowflake. Building the asset connects to the warehouse.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, config: SnowflakeConfig, password: str) -> None:\n        self._config = config\n        self._connection_string = build_connection_string(config, password)\n\n    def run_checks(\n        self,\n        *,\n        table: str,\n        schema: str | None,\n        checks: list[CheckSpec],\n    ) -> SuiteOutcome:\n        context = gx.get_context(mode=\"ephemeral\")\n        datasource = context.data_sources.add_snowflake(\n            name=f\"sf-{table}\",\n            connection_string=self._connection_string,\n        )\n        asset = datasource.add_table_asset(\n            name=table,\n            table_name=table,\n            schema_name=schema or self._config.schema_,\n        )\n        batch_definition = asset.add_batch_definition_whole_table(name=\"whole_table\")\n        suite = gx.ExpectationSuite(\n            name=f\"suite-{table}\",\n            expectations=[_to_gx_expectation(check) for check in checks],\n        )\n        validation_definition = gx.ValidationDefinition(\n            name=f\"vd-{table}\",\n            data=batch_definition,\n            suite=suite,\n        )\n        result = validation_definition.run(result_format=\"COMPLETE\")\n        return to_suite_outcome(result)\n\n\ndef build_snowflake_runner(\n    *,\n    config: dict[str, Any],\n    secret_ref: str | None,\n    secret_store: SecretStore,\n) -> SnowflakeCheckRunner:\n    \"\"\"Build a runner from a `Connection` row's config + secret_ref.\n\n    Takes primitives (not the ORM model) to keep the adapter decoupled from the\n    DB layer.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not secret_ref:\n        raise ValueError(\"Snowflake connection requires secret_ref for the password\")\n    sf_config = SnowflakeConfig.model_validate(config)\n    password = secret_store.get(secret_ref)\n    return SnowflakeCheckRunner(sf_config, password)\n"
}

10	Before committing and pushing changes, run the relevant local checks first. Do not rely on CI to discover format/lint/type/test failures.
11	
12	For frontend changes: `pnpm format:check && pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck` (and `pnpm test` once tests exist).
13	For backend changes: `black --check backend/ && ruff check backend/ && mypy backend/app/` (and `pytest` once tests exist).
14	
15	**Why:** Established after a CI-spam incident during PR #25 (PR1 tooling lock) where successive pushes each broke CI in a new way (Prettier on generated lockfile, ESLint v9 filename, TS6306/6310 on tsconfig.node.json). User said: "failed again, please check locally if possible before commit (we are spamming it now)." Re-confirmed after PR1 close-out: "going forward lets check in local when possible then."
16	
17	**How to apply:** Treat local green as the gate, push as the publish step. When a hook fails, fix the underlying issue — do not `--no-verify`. If a local check cannot reasonably be run (e.g., requires deployed Azure infra), say so explicitly rather than skipping silently.
18	
19	Related: [[dataq-working-agreements]], [[dataq-review-before-merge]]
20

If the real GX 1.17 result uses `.expectation_type` instead of `.type`, this `AttributeError`s against live Snowflake. > AGENT

**Real bug caught by the review** — and exactly the kind my fixture couldn't surface. GX 1.17's `ValidationDefinition.run()` requires the suite *and* the validation definition to be **registered on the context first** (`context.suites.add(...)` / `context.validation_definitions.add(...)`). My `run_checks` constructs them free-standing and calls `.run()` — that would raise `ValidationDefinitionRelatedResourcesFreshnessError` against live Snowflake. Let me confirm the fix and capture the result attribute shape in one go:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_016TDMHpRFFysVWPqvLbdgMa
```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ\ncat > _gx_shape.py <<'EOF'\nimport great_expectations as gx\nimport great_expectations.expectations as gxe\nimport pandas as pd\n\nctx = gx.get_context(mode=\"ephemeral\")\nds = ctx.data_sources.add_pandas(\"p\")\nasset = ds.add_dataframe_asset(\"a\")\nbd = asset.add_batch_definition_whole_dataframe(\"b\")\ndf = pd.DataFrame({\"id\": [1, 2, None]})\nsuite = ctx.suites.add(gx.ExpectationSuite(name=\"s\", expectations=[\n    gxe.ExpectColumnValuesToNotBeNull(column=\"id\"),\n    gxe.ExpectTableRowCountToBeBetween(min_value=1, max_value=10),\n]))\nvd = ctx.validation_definitions.add(gx.ValidationDefinition(name=\"vd\", data=bd, suite=suite))\nres = vd.run(batch_parameters={\"dataframe\": df}, result_format=\"COMPLETE\")\nprint(\"RUN OK. suite .success:\", res.success, type(res.success).__name__)\nr0 = res.results[0]\ncfg = r0.expectation_config\nprint(\"cfg .type:\", hasattr(cfg, \"type\"), \"->\", getattr(cfg, \"type\", \"X\"), \"| .expectation_type:\", hasattr(cfg, \"expectation_type\"))\nprint(\"cfg .kwargs:\", getattr(cfg, \"kwargs\", \"X\"))\nprint(\"r0.result keys:\", sorted(r0.result.keys()))\nprint(\"r0.success:\", r0.success)\nEOF\nconda run -n dataq python _gx_shape.py 2>&1 | tail -12\nrm -f _gx_shape.py",

}

> AGENT

Two confirmations from the real GX run:
1. **The registration fix works** — `ctx.suites.add(...)` + `ctx.validation_definitions.add(...)` makes `run()` succeed.
2. **`cfg.type` is correct** (`.expectation_type` doesn't exist) — so that part of my mapping was right.

But it also surfaced a **second, subtler bug**: real GX injects `batch_id` into `cfg.kwargs` (`{'batch_id': 'p-a', 'column': 'id'}`), so my `expected_value=dict(config.kwargs)` would persist GX's internal `batch_id` into the `results.expected_value` column. Fixing both in the adapter:

92	    """Map a GX ExpectationSuiteValidationResult onto our GX-agnostic DTO.

110	                sample_failures=_extract_sample_failures(detail),
111	            )
112	        )
113	    return SuiteOutcome(success=bool(gx_result.success), checks=outcomes)

## Where things stand

**PR 4a (#74)** — async backbone — ✅ CI green, review-clean (finding #1 fixed in-PR, Bandit fix applied, finding #2 → issue #75). **Ready to squash-merge.**

**PR 4b (#76)** — Snowflake GX adapter — ✅ CI green, review-clean. Self-review against real GX caught two bugs the fixture couldn't:
1. **(high)** `run()` would have failed on the first real Snowflake run — suite/validation now registered on the context.
2. **(low)** GX's internal `batch_id` was leaking into `results.expected_value` — now stripped.

Both fixed in `fb0ed4f`, verified against a real GX validation, documented on the PR.

**Process improvements banked this session:**
- Memory updated: local verification must include **Bandit** (`# noqa: S105` ≠ Bandit `B105`) — caused 4a's CI failure.
- Carry-forward noted for 4c: GX returns numpy `NaN` in `partial_unexpected_list`, which is invalid JSON for the `JSONB` columns — **4c persistence must sanitize `NaN`→`null`** (recorded on PR #76).

## Next steps — your call
4c depends on **both** 4a and 4b being on `main`. Two ways forward:

1. **Merge 4a + 4b now, then start 4c** (recommended) — both are green and independent; squash-merging them unblocks 4c cleanly off a fresh `main`.
2. **Start 4c stacked** on the 4b branch without merging — riskier (rebases if review changes 4a/4b).

Per your standing agreement I won't merge without your say-so. Want me to merge #74 and #76 (squash), then start PR 4c?

## So: no, we are *not* currently adding tests against the new code.

What I did for 4a/4b was **throwaway fixture smoke scripts** — I wrote them, ran them, and deleted them. That leaves the three new modules at 0% with zero regression protection.

**Is that a policy violation?** Strictly, no. The working agreements say tests are required *"from Week 8 onward"*, and *"manually test each committed change"* until then — which I did. The coverage gate is `--cov-fail-under=0` until Week 8. So we're within the letter of the rule.

**But I think we should add them anyway**, for three concrete reasons:
1. **The throwaway scripts caught three real bugs** (the eager-mode `request_id` clobber in 4a; the missing context-registration and `batch_id` leak in 4b). That test logic *already exists in my head and in this session* — deleting it threw away exactly the regression guard those bugs warrant.
2. **There's precedent for early tests** — PR 3b shipped the secret-service tests (88%) and 3c shipped frontend tests, both well before Week 8, because they were cheap.
3. **The GX-free parts are trivially, deterministically testable** with no Snowflake and no broker:
   - `celery_app.py` — the request_id inject/restore/clear signals incl. the eager-mode token restore
   - `snowflake.py` — config parsing, connection-string URL-encoding, expectation translation + `UnknownExpectationError`, `to_suite_outcome` against a real pandas GX result, `batch_id` stripping, the factory + missing-`secret_ref` guard

   That alone would take those three modules from 0% to ~80%+. Only `run_checks` against a live warehouse stays untested (the already-deferred follow-up).

How would you like to handle it?

"""Shared pytest fixtures."""

import os
from collections.abc import Iterator

# Set test-mode env vars BEFORE any backend.app.* import resolves. The auth
# module computes its mode at import time from settings; without these the
# TestClient lifespan would raise 'Auth not configured'.
os.environ.setdefault("ENVIRONMENT", "dev")
os.environ.setdefault("AUTH_DEV_BYPASS", "true")

import pytest

from backend.app.core import secrets
from backend.app.core.config import get_settings


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _reset_caches() -> Iterator[None]:
    """Clear cached singletons between tests so settings + secret store rebuild."""
    get_settings.cache_clear()
    secrets.reset_secret_store_cache()
    yield
    get_settings.cache_clear()
    secrets.reset_secret_store_cache()


@pytest.fixture
def clean_kv_env(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    """Strip every KV_SECRET_* env var so tests start from a clean slate."""
    import os

    for key in list(os.environ):
        if key.startswith("KV_SECRET_"):
            monkeypatch.delenv(key, raising=False)
=== test_secrets.py (head) ===
from types import SimpleNamespace

import pytest

from backend.app.core import secrets
from backend.app.core.secrets import (
    AzureKeyVaultStore,
    EnvSecretStore,
    SecretNotFoundError,
    _build_store,
    _env_key,
    get_secret_store,
)

# ───────────────────────── EnvSecretStore ──────────────────────────


def test_env_key_normalises_dashes_and_case() -> None:
    assert _env_key("snowflake-uat-finance") == "KV_SECRET_SNOWFLAKE_UAT_FINANCE"
    assert _env_key("adf-prod") == "KV_SECRET_ADF_PROD"
    assert _env_key("UPPER-already") == "KV_SECRET_UPPER_ALREADY"


def test_env_store_returns_value_when_set(
    clean_kv_env: None, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setenv("KV_SECRET_SNOWFLAKE_UAT_FINANCE", "s3cr3t")
    assert EnvSecretStore().get("snowflake-uat-finance") == "s3cr3t"


def test_env_store_raises_when_missing(clean_kv_env: None) -> None:
    with pytest.raises(SecretNotFoundError, match="KV_SECRET_MISSING"):
        EnvSecretStore().get("missing")


# ───────────────────────── AzureKeyVaultStore ──────────────────────


def test_akv_store_lazy_client_not_built_on_init() -> None:
    """Constructing the store must not import or build any Azure SDK client."""
    store = AzureKeyVaultStore("https://example.vault.azure.net/")
    assert store._client is None


def test_akv_store_get_returns_value(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    store = AzureKeyVaultStore("https://example.vault.azure.net/")
    fake_client = SimpleNamespace(get_secret=lambda name: SimpleNamespace(value="vault-value"))
    monkeypatch.setattr(store, "_client_lazy", lambda: fake_client)
    assert store.get("snowflake-uat-finance") == "vault-value"
```
