{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/worker/test_celery_app.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for the Celery app config and request_id propagation signals.\n\nNo broker is needed: the signal receivers are called directly with the same\narguments Celery would pass, and the `request_id_var` ContextVar is inspected\nto assert propagation. The eager-mode case guards the bug fixed pre-merge —\na blanket clear in task_postrun would drop the caller's request_id.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Iterator\nfrom types import SimpleNamespace\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import request_id_var\nfrom backend.app.worker.celery_app import (\n    REQUEST_ID_HEADER,\n    _clear_request_id,\n    _inject_request_id,\n    _restore_request_id,\n    create_celery_app,\n)\n\n\n@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)\ndef _clean_request_id() -> Iterator[None]:\n    \"\"\"Reset the ContextVar around each test so state never leaks between them.\"\"\"\n    request_id_var.set(None)\n    yield\n    request_id_var.set(None)\n\n\ndef _fake_task(request_id: str | None = None) -> SimpleNamespace:\n    \"\"\"A stand-in for a Celery task: only `.request` and its attrs are used.\"\"\"\n    request = SimpleNamespace()\n    if request_id is not None:\n        setattr(request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, request_id)\n    return SimpleNamespace(request=request)\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── app config ──────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_create_celery_app_uses_redis_url_and_json() -> None:\n    app = create_celery_app()\n    assert app.main == \"dataq\"\n    assert app.conf.broker_url.startswith(\"redis://\")\n    assert app.conf.result_backend.startswith(\"redis://\")\n    assert app.conf.task_serializer == \"json\"\n    assert app.conf.accept_content == [\"json\"]\n    # task_track_started lets the run read-back distinguish queued from running.\n    assert app.conf.task_track_started is True\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── inject (publisher side) ─────────────────\n\n\ndef test_inject_stamps_request_id_onto_headers_when_set() -> None:\n    request_id_var.set(\"req-123\")\n    headers: dict[str, str] = {}\n    _inject_request_id(headers=headers)\n    assert headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] == \"req-123\"\n\n\ndef test_inject_is_noop_when_request_id_unset() -> None:\n    headers: dict[str, str] = {}\n    _inject_request_id(headers=headers)\n    assert headers == {}\n\n\ndef test_inject_is_noop_when_headers_none() -> None:\n    request_id_var.set(\"req-123\")\n    # Must not raise when Celery passes headers=None.\n    _inject_request_id(headers=None)\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── restore / clear (worker side) ───────────\n\n\ndef test_restore_then_clear_in_worker_context() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Worker process starts uncorrelated: prerun sets, postrun clears to None.\"\"\"\n    task = _fake_task(\"req-abc\")\n    _restore_request_id(task=task)\n    assert request_id_var.get() == \"req-abc\"\n    _clear_request_id(task=task)\n    assert request_id_var.get() is None\n\n\ndef test_clear_restores_prior_value_under_eager_mode() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Eager mode: signals fire inside the caller's context.\n\n    postrun must restore the request's own request_id, not blow it away — the\n    regression this guards.\n    \"\"\"\n    request_id_var.set(\"req-CALLER\")\n    task = _fake_task(\"req-CALLER\")\n    _restore_request_id(task=task)\n    assert request_id_var.get() == \"req-CALLER\"\n    _clear_request_id(task=task)\n    assert request_id_var.get() == \"req-CALLER\"\n\n\ndef test_restore_is_noop_when_task_has_no_request_id() -> None:\n    \"\"\"A task dispatched without a request_id leaves the caller context intact.\"\"\"\n    request_id_var.set(\"req-CALLER\")\n    task = _fake_task(None)\n    _restore_request_id(task=task)\n    assert request_id_var.get() == \"req-CALLER\"\n    # postrun finds no stashed token, so it must not touch the var.\n    _clear_request_id(task=task)\n    assert request_id_var.get() == \"req-CALLER\"\n\n\ndef test_restore_handles_missing_task() -> None:\n    # Defensive: Celery always passes task, but the guard must hold regardless.\n    _restore_request_id(task=None)\n    assert request_id_var.get() is None\n"
}

expectation_config=SimpleNamespace(type=type_, kwargs=kwargs),\n        result=result,\n    )\n\n\ndef test_to_suite_outcome_maps_observed_and_expected() -> None:\n    gx_result = SimpleNamespace(\n        success=True,\n        results=[\n            _fake_check_result(\n                success=True,\n                type_=\"expect_table_row_count_to_be_between\",\n                kwargs={\"min_value\": 1, \"max_value\": 10},\n                result={\"observed_value\": 5},\n            )\n        ],\n    )\n    outcome = to_suite_outcome(gx_result)\n    assert outcome.success is True\n    (check,) = outcome.checks\n    assert check.observed_value == {\"observed_value\": 5}\n    assert check.expected_value == {\"min_value\": 1, \"max_value\": 10}\n    assert check.sample_failures is None\n\n\ndef test_to_suite_outcome_strips_gx_internal_batch_id() -> None:\n    gx_result = SimpleNamespace(\n        success=False,\n        results=[\n            _fake_check_result(\n                success=False,\n                type_=\"expect_column_values_to_not_be_null\",\n                kwargs={\"batch_id\": \"sf-t\", \"column\": \"id\"},\n                result={\"unexpected_count\": 2, \"partial_unexpected_list\": [None, None]},\n            )\n        ],\n    )\n    (check,) = to_suite_outcome(gx_result).checks\n    assert check.expected_value == {\"column\": \"id\"}  # batch_id removed\n    assert check.observed_value is None\n    assert check.sample_failures == {\"unexpected_count\": 2, \"partial_unexpected_list\": [None, None]}\n\n\ndef test_to_suite_outcome_against_real_gx_result() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Guards the GX 1.17 result shape (`.type`, injected `batch_id`) end-to-end.\"\"\"\n    import great_expectations as gx\n    import great_expectations.expectations as gxe\n    import pandas as pd\n\n    ctx = gx.get_context(mode=\"ephemeral\")\n    asset = ctx.data_sources.add_pandas(\"p\").add_dataframe_asset(\"a\")\n    batch_definition = asset.add_batch_definition_whole_dataframe(\"b\")\n    suite = ctx.suites.add(\n        gx.ExpectationSuite(\n            name=\"s\",\n            expectations=[\n                gxe.ExpectColumnValuesToNotBeNull(column=\"id\"),\n                gxe.ExpectTableRowCountToBeBetween(min_value=1, max_value=10),\n            ],\n        )\n    )\n    validation_definition = ctx.validation_definitions.add(\n        gx.ValidationDefinition(name=\"vd\", data=batch_definition, suite=suite)\n    )\n    gx_result = validation_definition.run(\n        batch_parameters={\"dataframe\": pd.DataFrame({\"id\": [1, 2, None]})},\n        result_format=\"COMPLETE\",\n    )\n\n    outcome = to_suite_outcome(gx_result)\n    assert outcome.success is False\n    by_type = {c.expectation_type: c for c in outcome.checks}\n    not_null = by_type[\"expect_column_values_to_not_be_null\"]\n    assert not_null.success is False\n    assert not_null.expected_value == {\"column\": \"id\"}  # no batch_id leak\n    assert not_null.sample_failures[\"unexpected_count\"] == 1\n    row_count = by_type[\"expect_table_row_count_to_be_between\"]\n    assert row_count.success is True\n    assert row_count.observed_value == {\"observed_value\": 3}\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── factory ─────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_build_runner_resolves_secret_and_returns_check_runner() -> None:\n    store = _FakeStore()\n    runner = build_snowflake_runner(config=_CONFIG, secret_ref=\"snowflake-dev\", secret_store=store)\n    assert isinstance(runner, SnowflakeCheckRunner)\n    assert isinstance(runner, CheckRunner)  # satisfies the Protocol\n    assert store.asked == \"snowflake-dev\"\n\n\ndef test_build_runner_requires_secret_ref() -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=\"secret_ref\"):\n        build_snowflake_runner(config=_CONFIG, secret_ref=None, secret_store=_FakeStore())\n"

> AGENT

**Critical catch before pushing** — and exactly the "verify locally, don't let CI be the first feedback loop" lesson. The CI "Backend — tests" job installs only pytest tools + `requirements-typecheck.txt`. It does **not** install `celery`, `great-expectations`, or `pandas`. My new test modules import `backend.app.worker.celery_app` (→ celery) and `backend.app.datasources.snowflake` (→ GX/pandas) **at module top**, so CI test collection would fail on import — even though everything passes in my full conda env.

The existing tests never imported celery/GX, so the minimal CI env sufficed. Now that we're testing the worker + datasource code, CI must have those deps. This is groundwork the whole rest of the project needs anyway (every Week 2+ datasource/execution test will import GX).

My **local** verification command broke on fish's globbing of `celery[redis]` (`no matches found`) — so celery/GX never installed in the venv, causing the collection errors. Important: that's a fish-shell artifact of *my* test command, **not** a CI problem — GitHub Actions `run:` steps use bash, where `celery[redis]==5.6.3` is fine and needs no quoting. So `ci.yml` is correct; my replication command just needs quotes. Re-running the install (quoted) into the existing venv:

## What this surfaced (and I handled)
- **CI test job was missing celery + GX** — my tests import them at module load, so CI collection would have failed. Fixed in `ci.yml` and **verified in a clean pip-only venv mirroring CI** (not just my conda env) — the right way to validate a CI change.
- **Posted a non-blocking follow-up observation**: the celery/GX pins are now duplicated (`environment.yml` ↔ `ci.yml`) — same drift class as #54/#68. Candidate for the deps-sync guard.

## Going forward
The throwaway-smoke pattern was the gap. From here, GX-free logic gets real pytest tests in its own PR (or folded into the feature PR), and live-Snowflake/broker paths stay deferred to Week 8's mocking fixtures — matching the roadmap.

> AGENT

Here's the PR 4c commit breakdown. It closes the Week 1 exit gate and folds in the three carry-forwards (#75, NaN→null, tests-per-commit). First, two facts that shape it:

- **No DB migration needed** — `Run`, `Result`, `Suite`, `Check`, `Connection`, `User` all exist in the baseline. 4c is pure code + seed data.
- **"Persists a result row" without live creds:** per our earlier decision (injectable adapter, real-Snowflake smoke deferred), the service/task/endpoint are built against the real `build_snowflake_runner` factory but **unit-tested with a fake `CheckRunner`**. With no Snowflake creds the live probe fails-soft (Run → `failed`, error persisted); the green run against Snowflake DEV is the deferred follow-up (same pattern as deferred real-AAD). So the *plumbing* closes the gate and is fully tested; the literal live-green run waits on creds.

## Proposed commits (each ships green, with its own tests)

**1 · `feat(core): JSON-safe sanitizer (NaN/Inf → null)`**
- `core/jsonsafe.py`: recursively walk dict/list, replace `float('nan')`/`inf`/`-inf` with `None` so GX values are valid JSON before hitting `JSONB`.
- Tests: `nan`/`inf` → `null`, nested structures, the real `partial_unexpected_list: [nan]` shape. *(the NaN→null carry-forward)*

**2 · `feat(services): GX run/result persistence service`**
- `services/run_service.py`: `execute_run(session, run, checks, runner)` — flips Run `queued`→`running`, calls `runner.run_checks(...)`, maps each `CheckOutcome`→`Result` row (status `passed`/`failed`, observed/expected/sample through the commit-1 sanitizer), sets `succeeded`/`failed` + `started_at`/`finished_at`, commits. On runner exception → Run `failed`.
- Runner is **injected** (the `CheckRunner` Protocol), so tests use a fake — no GX/Snowflake.
- Tests: success path, failed-check path, runner-raises path, NaN sanitization end-to-end, timestamps/status transitions.
- *Note:* outcome→check mapping is positional (GX preserves suite order; I verified). Robust check_id mapping via expectation `meta` is a Week 3 item when check CRUD lands — I'll leave a comment.

**3 · `feat(worker): run_suite Celery task`**
- `worker/tasks.py`: `@celery_app.task run_suite(run_id)` — opens a DB session, loads Run/Suite/Checks/Connection, builds the runner via `build_snowflake_runner(connection.config, connection.secret_ref, get_secret_store())`, calls `run_service.execute_run`. Registered on the app (import in `celery_app` or autodiscover).
- Tests: eager mode + monkeypatched factory → fake runner; assert Run reaches `succeeded` and Result rows exist.

**4 · `feat(api): _probe/snowflake-suite endpoint + run read-back`**
- `api/v1/probe.py`: `POST /api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite` (auth-protected) — idempotent seed of the dev User + Snowflake `Connection` + canned `Suite`/`Check`s, create `Run(queued)`, `run_suite.delay(run_id)`, return `{run_id, status}`. `GET /api/v1/_probe/runs/{run_id}` → run status + results. Pydantic response models + Swagger tags. Router wired in `main.py`.
- Tests: happy path with fake runner (eager) → 200 + run_id; GET returns persisted results; 401 when auth not bypassed.

**5 · `test(worker): request_id propagates FastAPI→Celery (Fixes #75)`**
- Dispatch `run_suite` eagerly with a known `request_id`, capture worker log output, assert the log line carries it. Closes **#75**.

**6 · `docs(progress): close Week 1 exit gate; advance to Week 2`**
- Flip the 3 Week-1 GX tasks ✅; mark W5 "Celery runner" / W7 "containerise" 🟡 (early, with PR links); record the early test credit; bump snapshot + aggregate + **CLAUDE.md §13 → Week 2**.

## My default decisions (flag if you'd change any)
- **Dev probe connection config** comes from new **`Settings` fields** (12-factor, consistent with `config.py`): `probe_snowflake_account/user/database/schema/warehouse/role/table` + `secret_ref` (password via `SecretStore`/`KV_SECRET_*`). The canned suite = one `expect_table_row_count_to_be_between` + one `expect_column_values_to_not_be_null`.
- **Real-Snowflake run deferred** (fails-soft without creds); I'll file the live-smoke follow-up issue in commit 4.

One decision for you:

=== session.py ===
from collections.abc import Iterator

from sqlalchemy import create_engine
from sqlalchemy.engine import Engine
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session, sessionmaker

from backend.app.core.config import get_settings


def _build_engine() -> Engine:
    settings = get_settings()
    return create_engine(settings.database_url, pool_pre_ping=True, future=True)


engine: Engine = _build_engine()
SessionLocal = sessionmaker(bind=engine, autoflush=False, autocommit=False, future=True)


def get_session() -> Session:
    return SessionLocal()


def get_db() -> Iterator[Session]:
    db = SessionLocal()
    try:
        yield db
    finally:
        db.close()
```

> AGENT

GX returns **native Python types** (`int`/`float`), not numpy scalars — the only JSONB-hostile value is `nan` (`json.dumps` emits the invalid `NaN` token). So the sanitizer stays tight: non-finite floats → `None`, recurse dict/list. No numpy handling needed.

Writing commit 1 — the sanitizer + its tests:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01SFYDH3qwP3TChpSAGEJvbk
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/core/jsonsafe.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Make values safe to persist into PostgreSQL ``JSONB`` columns.\n\nGreat Expectations reports non-finite floats (``NaN``, ``Infinity``) inside\nresult payloads — e.g. a ``partial_unexpected_list`` of failing values, or an\n``unexpected_percent`` on an empty batch. Python's ``json`` renders these as the\nbare tokens ``NaN`` / ``Infinity``, which are not valid JSON and which Postgres\n``JSONB`` rejects. ``sanitize_json`` walks a structure and replaces every\nnon-finite float with ``None`` so GX results round-trip cleanly into the\n``results`` table.\n\nGX 1.17 returns native Python scalars (not numpy types), so only ``float`` needs\nspecial handling here.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport math\nfrom typing import Any\n\n\ndef sanitize_json(value: Any) -> Any:\n    \"\"\"Recursively replace non-finite floats with ``None``; leave the rest intact.\n\n    Containers are rebuilt (dicts/lists); tuples become lists so the result is\n    JSON-native. Scalars other than non-finite floats pass through unchanged.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(value, float):\n        return value if math.isfinite(value) else None\n    if isinstance(value, dict):\n        return {key: sanitize_json(item) for key, item in value.items()}\n    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):\n        return [sanitize_json(item) for item in value]\n    return value\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/core/test_jsonsafe.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for the JSONB sanitizer.\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport math\n\nfrom backend.app.core.jsonsafe import sanitize_json\n\n\ndef test_nan_becomes_none() -> None:\n    assert sanitize_json(float(\"nan\")) is None\n\n\ndef test_infinities_become_none() -> None:\n    assert sanitize_json(float(\"inf\")) is None\n    assert sanitize_json(float(\"-inf\")) is None\n\n\ndef test_finite_floats_pass_through() -> None:\n    assert sanitize_json(0.5) == 0.5\n    assert sanitize_json(0.0) == 0.0\n    assert sanitize_json(-3.14) == -3.14\n\n\ndef test_non_floats_pass_through() -> None:\n    assert sanitize_json(3) == 3\n    assert sanitize_json(\"id\") == \"id\"\n    assert sanitize_json(True) is True\n    assert sanitize_json(None) is None\n\n\ndef test_nested_structure_is_sanitized() -> None:\n    # Mirrors a real GX result fragment: a sample list with a NaN.\n    payload = {\n        \"unexpected_count\": 1,\n        \"unexpected_percent\": float(\"nan\"),\n        \"partial_unexpected_list\": [None, float(\"nan\"), 2.0],\n        \"observed_value\": 3,\n    }\n    cleaned = sanitize_json(payload)\n    assert cleaned == {\n        \"unexpected_count\": 1,\n        \"unexpected_percent\": None,\n        \"partial_unexpected_list\": [None, None, 2.0],\n        \"observed_value\": 3,\n    }\n\n\ndef test_result_is_strict_json_serialisable() -> None:\n    \"\"\"The whole point: output must serialise with allow_nan=False (JSONB-safe).\"\"\"\n    payload = {\"sample\": [float(\"nan\"), float(\"inf\")], \"n\": 2}\n    cleaned = sanitize_json(payload)\n    # allow_nan=False raises ValueError if any NaN/Infinity survived.\n    json.dumps(cleaned, allow_nan=False)\n\n\ndef test_tuples_become_lists() -> None:\n    result = sanitize_json((1.0, float(\"nan\"), 3))\n    assert result == [1.0, None, 3]\n\n\ndef test_does_not_mutate_input() -> None:\n    original = {\"x\": [float(\"nan\")]}\n    sanitize_json(original)\n    assert math.isnan(original[\"x\"][0])  # input untouched\n"
}

> AGENT

GX returns native types — sanitizer scope confirmed tight. Commit 1 in (`990e4fd`). Now commit 2 — the run service.

Key design decisions baked in:
- **Run.status** reflects *execution* (`succeeded` = ran to completion, even if checks failed; `failed` = runner raised). Check pass/fail lives in `Result.status` + `SuiteOutcome.success`.
- Runner is **injected** (`CheckRunner` Protocol) → tests use a fake, no GX/Snowflake/DB.
- Fallible work (run_checks + building rows) is isolated so a failure can't leave partial `Result` inserts.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/run_service.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Execute a suite's checks via a datasource adapter and persist the results.\n\nThis is the GX-agnostic core of a run: it drives the `Run` lifecycle, calls a\n`CheckRunner` (injected — Snowflake in production, a fake in tests), and maps the\nreturned `SuiteOutcome` onto `Result` rows. GX/Snowflake specifics live behind\nthe adapter; this layer only knows the DTOs in ``datasources.base``.\n\nRun.status describes *execution*, not data quality: a run that completes is\n``succeeded`` even when checks fail (the failures live in ``Result.status`` /\n``SuiteOutcome.success``). ``failed`` means the run could not execute — the\nadapter raised (e.g. could not reach the warehouse).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom backend.app.core.jsonsafe import sanitize_json\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import get_logger\nfrom backend.app.datasources.base import CheckRunner, CheckSpec\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Check, Result, Run\n\nlog = get_logger(__name__)\n\n\ndef _now() -> datetime:\n    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n\n\ndef execute_run(\n    session: Session,\n    *,\n    run: Run,\n    checks: list[Check],\n    runner: CheckRunner,\n    table: str,\n    schema: str | None = None,\n) -> Run:\n    \"\"\"Run ``checks`` against ``table`` via ``runner`` and persist the outcome.\n\n    ``run`` must already be persisted (it carries the id the results link to).\n    Returns the same `Run`, updated to ``succeeded`` or ``failed``.\n    \"\"\"\n    run.status = \"running\"\n    run.started_at = _now()\n    session.commit()\n    log.info(\n        \"run_started\",\n        run_id=str(run.id),\n        suite_id=str(run.suite_id),\n        n_checks=len(checks),\n        table=table,\n    )\n\n    specs = [CheckSpec(expectation_type=c.expectation_type, kwargs=dict(c.config)) for c in checks]\n\n    # Fallible work first; only persist results if it all succeeds, so a failure\n    # can never leave a half-written set of Result rows.\n    try:\n        outcome = runner.run_checks(table=table, schema=schema, checks=specs)\n        rows = [\n            Result(\n                run_id=run.id,\n                check_id=check.id,\n                status=\"passed\" if check_outcome.success else \"failed\",\n                observed_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.observed_value),\n                expected_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.expected_value),\n                sample_failures=sanitize_json(check_outcome.sample_failures),\n            )\n            for check, check_outcome in zip(checks, outcome.checks, strict=True)\n        ]\n    except Exception:\n        run.status = \"failed\"\n        run.finished_at = _now()\n        session.commit()\n        log.exception(\"run_failed\", run_id=str(run.id), table=table)\n        return run\n\n    session.add_all(rows)\n    run.status = \"succeeded\"\n    run.finished_at = _now()\n    session.commit()\n    log.info(\n        \"run_completed\",\n        run_id=str(run.id),\n        suite_success=outcome.success,\n        n_results=len(rows),\n    )\n    return run\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/services/test_run_service.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for the run/result persistence service.\n\nNo database or GX: a fake Session records what would be persisted, model\ninstances are built in memory with explicit ids, and a fake CheckRunner returns\ncanned outcomes (or raises). This keeps the service's lifecycle + mapping logic\nunder test independent of Postgres and Snowflake.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport uuid\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Check, Result, Run\nfrom backend.app.services import run_service\n\n\nclass FakeSession:\n    \"\"\"Records add_all'd rows and counts commits; everything else is a no-op.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self.added: list[Result] = []\n        self.commits = 0\n\n    def add_all(self, rows: list[Result]) -> None:\n        self.added.extend(rows)\n\n    def commit(self) -> None:\n        self.commits += 1\n\n\nclass FakeRunner:\n    def __init__(self, outcome: SuiteOutcome | None = None, raises: Exception | None = None) -> None:\n        self._outcome = outcome\n        self._raises = raises\n        self.called_with: dict[str, object] | None = None\n\n    def run_checks(\n        self, *, table: str, schema: str | None, checks: list[CheckSpec]\n    ) -> SuiteOutcome:\n        self.called_with = {\"table\": table, \"schema\": schema, \"checks\": checks}\n        if self._raises is not None:\n            raise self._raises\n        assert self._outcome is not None\n        return self._outcome\n\n\ndef _run() -> Run:\n    return Run(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=uuid.uuid4(), status=\"queued\")\n\n\ndef _checks(n: int) -> list[Check]:\n    return [\n        Check(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=uuid.uuid4(), name=f\"c{i}\", expectation_type=\"x\", config={})\n        for i in range(n)\n    ]\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── success path ────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_successful_run_persists_results_and_marks_succeeded() -> None:\n    session = FakeSession()\n    run = _run()\n    checks = _checks(2)\n    outcome = SuiteOutcome(\n        success=False,  # a check failed, but the RUN still executed\n        checks=[\n            CheckOutcome(\"expect_a\", success=True, observed_value={\"observed_value\": 5}),\n            CheckOutcome(\n                \"expect_b\",\n                success=False,\n                expected_value={\"column\": \"id\"},\n                sample_failures={\"unexpected_count\": 1},\n            ),\n        ],\n    )\n    runner = FakeRunner(outcome=outcome)\n\n    result = run_service.execute_run(\n        session, run=run, checks=checks, runner=runner, table=\"ORDERS\", schema=\"FIN\"\n    )\n\n    assert result is run\n    assert run.status == \"succeeded\"  # ran to completion despite a failed check\n    assert run.started_at is not None and run.finished_at is not None\n    assert len(session.added) == 2\n    statuses = {r.check_id: r.status for r in session.added}\n    assert statuses[checks[0].id] == \"passed\"\n    assert statuses[checks[1].id] == \"failed\"\n    # adapter received specs derived from the checks + the target table\n    assert runner.called_with == {\n        \"table\": \"ORDERS\",\n        \"schema\": \"FIN\",\n        \"checks\": [CheckSpec(\"x\", {}), CheckSpec(\"x\", {})],\n    }\n\n\ndef test_results_link_to_run_and_check_ids() -> None:\n    session = FakeSession()\n    run = _run()\n    checks = _checks(1)\n    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome(\"x\", success=True)]))\n\n    run_service.execute_run(session, run=run, checks=checks, runner=runner, table=\"T\")\n\n    (row,) = session.added\n    assert row.run_id == run.id\n    assert row.check_id == checks[0].id\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── NaN sanitisation ────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_nan_in_sample_failures_is_sanitised_before_persist() -> None:\n    session = FakeSession()\n    runner = FakeRunner(\n        SuiteOutcome(\n            success=False,\n            checks=[\n                CheckOutcome(\n                    \"x\",\n                    success=False,\n                    sample_failures={\"partial_unexpected_list\": [float(\"nan\"), 2.0]},\n                )\n            ],\n        )\n    )\n\n    run_service.execute_run(session, run=_run(), checks=_checks(1), runner=runner, table=\"T\")\n\n    (row,) = session.added\n    assert row.sample_failures == {\"partial_unexpected_list\": [None, 2.0]}\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── failure path ────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_runner_exception_marks_failed_and_persists_no_results() -> None:\n    session = FakeSession()\n    run = _run()\n    runner = FakeRunner(raises=RuntimeError(\"cannot reach warehouse\"))\n\n    result = run_service.execute_run(session, run=run, checks=_checks(2), runner=runner, table=\"T\")\n\n    assert result.status == \"failed\"\n    assert run.finished_at is not None\n    assert session.added == []  # no half-written results\n\n\ndef test_outcome_count_mismatch_marks_failed() -> None:\n    \"\"\"zip(strict=True): if the adapter returns the wrong number of outcomes.\"\"\"\n    session = FakeSession()\n    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome(\"x\", success=True)]))\n\n    run_service.execute_run(session, run=_run(), checks=_checks(3), runner=runner, table=\"T\")\n\n    assert session.added == []\n\n\ndef test_empty_run_still_succeeds() -> None:\n    session = FakeSession()\n    run = _run()\n    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[]))\n\n    run_service.execute_run(session, run=run, checks=[], runner=runner, table=\"T\")\n\n    assert run.status == \"succeeded\"\n    assert session.added == []\n"
}

> AGENT

PR 4c-i is open (#78). Reviewing it now — and one real robustness gap stands out in `execute_run`: if `runner.run_checks` **succeeds** but the subsequent `session.add_all`/`commit` raises (DB drop, constraint), that's outside the `try`, so the run is left stuck in `"running"` with no terminal state — a real wart for a monitoring product (perpetual "running" on dashboards). Let me restructure so any failure after the running-checkpoint marks the run `failed`.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/run_service.py",
  "old_string": "    specs = [CheckSpec(expectation_type=c.expectation_type, kwargs=dict(c.config)) for c in checks]\n\n    # Fallible work first; only persist results if it all succeeds, so a failure\n    # can never leave a half-written set of Result rows.\n    try:\n        outcome = runner.run_checks(table=table, schema=schema, checks=specs)\n        rows = [\n            Result(\n                run_id=run.id,\n                check_id=check.id,\n                status=\"passed\" if check_outcome.success else \"failed\",\n                observed_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.observed_value),\n                expected_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.expected_value),\n                sample_failures=sanitize_json(check_outcome.sample_failures),\n            )\n            for check, check_outcome in zip(checks, outcome.checks, strict=True)\n        ]\n    except Exception:\n        run.status = \"failed\"\n        run.finished_at = _now()\n        session.commit()\n        log.exception(\"run_failed\", run_id=str(run.id), table=table)\n        return run\n\n    session.add_all(rows)\n    run.status = \"succeeded\"\n    run.finished_at = _now()\n    session.commit()\n    log.info(\n        \"run_completed\",\n        run_id=str(run.id),\n        suite_success=outcome.success,\n        n_results=len(rows),\n    )\n    return run",
  "new_string": "    specs = [CheckSpec(expectation_type=c.expectation_type, kwargs=dict(c.config)) for c in checks]\n\n    # Everything from here — running the adapter, building rows, and persisting\n    # them — is guarded so any failure drives the run to a terminal 'failed'\n    # state. Without this, a DB error during add_all/commit would leave the run\n    # stuck in 'running' forever. rollback() discards any partial result inserts\n    # before we record the failure.\n    try:\n        outcome = runner.run_checks(table=table, schema=schema, checks=specs)\n        rows = [\n            Result(\n                run_id=run.id,\n                check_id=check.id,\n                status=\"passed\" if check_outcome.success else \"failed\",\n                observed_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.observed_value),\n                expected_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.expected_value),\n                sample_failures=sanitize_json(check_outcome.sample_failures),\n            )\n            for check, check_outcome in zip(checks, outcome.checks, strict=True)\n        ]\n        session.add_all(rows)\n        run.status = \"succeeded\"\n        run.finished_at = _now()\n        session.commit()\n    except Exception:\n        session.rollback()\n        run.status = \"failed\"\n        run.finished_at = _now()\n        session.commit()\n        log.exception(\"run_failed\", run_id=str(run.id), table=table)\n        return run\n\n    log.info(\n        \"run_completed\",\n        run_id=str(run.id),\n        suite_success=outcome.success,\n        n_results=len(rows),\n    )\n    return run"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/services/test_run_service.py",
  "old_string": "class FakeSession:\n    \"\"\"Records add_all'd rows and counts commits; everything else is a no-op.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self.added: list[Result] = []\n        self.commits = 0\n\n    def add_all(self, rows: list[Result]) -> None:\n        self.added.extend(rows)\n\n    def commit(self) -> None:\n        self.commits += 1",
  "new_string": "class FakeSession:\n    \"\"\"Records add_all'd rows; counts commits/rollbacks. `add_all_raises` simulates\n    a persistence failure (e.g. DB error) after the adapter has already run.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, *, add_all_raises: Exception | None = None) -> None:\n        self.added: list[Result] = []\n        self.commits = 0\n        self.rollbacks = 0\n        self._add_all_raises = add_all_raises\n\n    def add_all(self, rows: list[Result]) -> None:\n        if self._add_all_raises is not None:\n            raise self._add_all_raises\n        self.added.extend(rows)\n\n    def commit(self) -> None:\n        self.commits += 1\n\n    def rollback(self) -> None:\n        self.rollbacks += 1"
}

}

3. `test(worker): request_id propagates FastAPI→Celery` — **Fixes #75**
4. `docs(progress): close Week 1 exit gate → Week 2` (+ CLAUDE.md §13)

from datetime import datetime
from typing import Annotated
from uuid import UUID

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict

from backend.app.core.auth import get_current_user
from backend.app.db.models import User

router = APIRouter(tags=["auth"])


class MeResponse(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)

    id: UUID
    aad_object_id: str
    email: str
    display_name: str | None
    last_seen_at: datetime | None


@router.get("/me", response_model=MeResponse)
def me(current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)]) -> User:
    return current_user

> AGENT

Auth pattern is clear: `get_current_user` returns a persisted `User` (dev-bypass upserts one), so the endpoint gets `current_user.id` for `created_by`, with `get_db` for the session.

One scoping decision I'll make rather than belabor: **4c-ii tests use fakes (fake session + fake runner), not a real Postgres.** CI has no Postgres service, and the roadmap explicitly schedules DB-backed/integration fixtures for Week 8. This matches how `run_service` was tested and keeps tests runnable everywhere. I'll note the Week 8 real-DB integration follow-up. To make the task testable that way, I'll split a testable `_run_suite(session, ...)` core from the thin `@celery_app.task` wrapper.

Also: the suite has no target-table column yet (Week 3 modeling), so `run_suite(run_id, table, schema)` takes the table as an arg — the probe endpoint supplies it from settings. Let me start commit 1 (config + task). Reading the exact config insertion point:

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/worker/tasks.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Celery tasks for asynchronous suite execution.\n\n``run_suite`` is the worker entry point dispatched by the probe endpoint. It\nloads the run's suite / connection / checks, builds the datasource adapter, and\nhands off to ``run_service.execute_run``. The DB-touching core is factored into\n``_run_suite`` so it can be unit-tested with a fake session + fake runner (no\nPostgres, no Snowflake); real-DB integration coverage is a Week 8 item.\n\nThe target table is passed in by the caller: the suite/dataset model does not\ncarry a target table until Week 3, so for now the probe endpoint supplies it.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport uuid\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import select\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import get_logger\nfrom backend.app.core.secrets import get_secret_store\nfrom backend.app.datasources.snowflake import build_snowflake_runner\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Check, Connection, Run, Suite\nfrom backend.app.db.session import get_session\nfrom backend.app.services import run_service\nfrom backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app\n\nlog = get_logger(__name__)\n\n\ndef _run_suite(session: Session, *, run_id: uuid.UUID, table: str, schema: str | None) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Load the run's graph, build the runner, execute. Returns the final status.\n\n    Failures while loading or building the runner (missing rows, bad connection\n    config, unresolved secret) drive the run to ``failed`` so it never lingers in\n    ``queued``; execution failures are handled inside ``execute_run``.\n    \"\"\"\n    run = session.get(Run, run_id)\n    if run is None:\n        log.error(\"run_suite_run_not_found\", run_id=str(run_id))\n        return \"not_found\"\n\n    try:\n        suite = session.get(Suite, run.suite_id)\n        connection = session.get(Connection, suite.connection_id) if suite is not None else None\n        if suite is None or connection is None:\n            raise RuntimeError(\"suite or connection not found for run\")\n        checks = list(session.scalars(select(Check).where(Check.suite_id == suite.id)))\n        runner = build_snowflake_runner(\n            config=connection.config,\n            secret_ref=connection.secret_ref,\n            secret_store=get_secret_store(),\n        )\n    except Exception:\n        run.status = \"failed\"\n        run.started_at = run.started_at or datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n        run.finished_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n        session.commit()\n        log.exception(\"run_suite_setup_failed\", run_id=str(run_id))\n        return \"failed\"\n\n    run_service.execute_run(\n        session, run=run, checks=checks, runner=runner, table=table, schema=schema\n    )\n    return str(run.status)\n\n\n@celery_app.task(name=\"run_suite\")  # type: ignore[misc]  # celery task decorator is unannotated\ndef run_suite(run_id: str, table: str, schema: str | None = None) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Worker entry point. ``run_id`` is a string so it serialises over JSON.\"\"\"\n    session = get_session()\n    try:\n        return _run_suite(session, run_id=uuid.UUID(run_id), table=table, schema=schema)\n    finally:\n        session.close()\n"
}

35	def create_celery_app() -> Celery:
36	    settings = get_settings()
37	    app = Celery(
38	        "dataq",
39	        broker=settings.redis_url,
40	        backend=settings.redis_url,

44	        result_serializer="json",

46	        timezone="UTC",

48	        # Surface a 'started' state so the run-status read-back can distinguish
49	        # queued from running without waiting for completion.
50	        task_track_started=True,
51	    )
52	    return app

58	@setup_logging.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
59	def _configure_celery_logging(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:
60	    """Disable Celery's default logging and route worker logs through structlog.
61	
62	    Connecting any receiver to ``setup_logging`` tells Celery not to configure
63	    logging itself, so our JSON + PII-redacting processor chain stays in force
64	    inside the worker exactly as it is in the API.
65	    """
66	    configure_logging()
67	
68	
69	@before_task_publish.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
70	def _inject_request_id(headers: dict[str, Any] | None = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
71	    """Caller side (FastAPI): stamp the active request_id onto task headers."""
72	    if headers is None:
73	        return
74	    rid = request_id_var.get()
75	    if rid is not None:
76	        headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = rid
77	
78	
79	@task_prerun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
80	def _restore_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
81	    """Worker side: restore request_id from the message into the ContextVar.
82	
83	    Custom headers added in ``before_task_publish`` are exposed as attributes on
84	    ``task.request`` under the protocol-v2 message format. We stash the reset
85	    token on ``task.request`` so ``task_postrun`` can restore the *prior* value
86	    rather than blindly clearing — under ``task_always_eager`` these signals run
87	    in the caller's context, so a blanket reset would drop the request_id for
88	    the rest of the request handler.
89	    """
90	    rid = getattr(task.request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, None) if task is not None else None
91	    if rid and task is not None:
92	        token = request_id_var.set(rid)
93	        setattr(task.request, _REQUEST_ID_RESET_ATTR, token)
94	
95	
96	@task_postrun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
97	def _clear_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
98	    """Worker side: restore the ContextVar to its pre-task value.
99	
100	    Only resets when ``task_prerun`` actually set it (token present), mirroring
101	    the ``reset(token)`` pattern used by ``request_id_middleware`` in main.py.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/worker/test_tasks.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for the run_suite Celery task orchestration.\n\nNo Postgres, no GX, no broker: a fake Session serves the run graph from memory,\n``build_snowflake_runner`` is monkeypatched to a fake CheckRunner, and the task\ncore (`_run_suite`) is called directly. Real-DB integration coverage is a Week 8\nitem (Postgres test fixtures).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport uuid\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Check, Connection, Run, Suite\nfrom backend.app.worker import tasks\n\n\nclass FakeSession:\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        run: Run | None = None,\n        suite: Suite | None = None,\n        connection: Connection | None = None,\n        checks: tuple[Check, ...] = (),\n    ) -> None:\n        self._objs: dict[type, Any] = {Run: run, Suite: suite, Connection: connection}\n        self._checks = list(checks)\n        self.added: list[Any] = []\n        self.commits = 0\n        self.rollbacks = 0\n        self.closed = False\n\n    def get(self, model: type, pk: Any) -> Any:\n        return self._objs.get(model)\n\n    def scalars(self, _stmt: Any) -> Any:\n        return iter(self._checks)\n\n    def add_all(self, rows: list[Any]) -> None:\n        self.added.extend(rows)\n\n    def commit(self) -> None:\n        self.commits += 1\n\n    def rollback(self) -> None:\n        self.rollbacks += 1\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        self.closed = True\n\n\nclass FakeRunner:\n    def __init__(self, outcome: SuiteOutcome) -> None:\n        self._outcome = outcome\n\n    def run_checks(\n        self, *, table: str, schema: str | None, checks: list[CheckSpec]\n    ) -> SuiteOutcome:\n        return self._outcome\n\n\ndef _graph(n_checks: int = 1) -> tuple[Run, Suite, Connection, tuple[Check, ...]]:\n    suite_id = uuid.uuid4()\n    conn_id = uuid.uuid4()\n    user_id = uuid.uuid4()\n    run = Run(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=suite_id, status=\"queued\")\n    suite = Suite(id=suite_id, name=\"probe\", connection_id=conn_id, created_by=user_id)\n    connection = Connection(\n        id=conn_id,\n        name=\"probe-sf\",\n        type=\"snowflake\",\n        env=\"dev\",\n        config={\"account\": \"a\"},\n        secret_ref=\"snowflake-dev\",\n        created_by=user_id,\n    )\n    checks = tuple(\n        Check(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=suite_id, name=f\"c{i}\", expectation_type=\"x\", config={})\n        for i in range(n_checks)\n    )\n    return run, suite, connection, checks\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── happy path ──────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_run_suite_executes_and_persists(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:\n    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(2)\n    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)\n    runner = FakeRunner(\n        SuiteOutcome(\n            success=True,\n            checks=[CheckOutcome(\"x\", success=True), CheckOutcome(\"x\", success=True)],\n        )\n    )\n    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, \"build_snowflake_runner\", lambda **_kw: runner)\n\n    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id, table=\"ORDERS\", schema=None)\n\n    assert status == \"succeeded\"\n    assert run.status == \"succeeded\"\n    assert len(session.added) == 2\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── failure paths ───────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_run_suite_missing_run_returns_not_found() -> None:\n    session = FakeSession(run=None)\n    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=uuid.uuid4(), table=\"T\", schema=None)\n    assert status == \"not_found\"\n\n\ndef test_run_suite_missing_connection_marks_failed() -> None:\n    run, suite, _conn, checks = _graph(1)\n    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=None, checks=checks)\n    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id, table=\"T\", schema=None)\n    assert status == \"failed\"\n    assert run.status == \"failed\"\n    assert run.finished_at is not None\n    assert session.added == []\n\n\ndef test_run_suite_runner_build_failure_marks_failed(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:\n    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)\n    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)\n\n    def _boom(**_kw: Any) -> Any:\n        raise ValueError(\"Snowflake connection requires secret_ref for the password\")\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, \"build_snowflake_runner\", _boom)\n\n    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id, table=\"T\", schema=None)\n    assert status == \"failed\"\n    assert run.status == \"failed\"\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── task wrapper ────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_task_wrapper_opens_and_closes_session(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:\n    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)\n    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)\n    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome(\"x\", success=True)]))\n    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, \"get_session\", lambda: session)\n    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, \"build_snowflake_runner\", lambda **_kw: runner)\n\n    status = tasks.run_suite(str(run.id), \"ORDERS\")\n\n    assert status == \"succeeded\"\n    assert session.closed is True\n"
}

The probe endpoint and its idempotent seed (get-or-create Connection/Suite/Checks, create Run) are inherently **DB-integration code**. The models use Postgres-specific types (`JSONB`, `UUID`, `gen_random_uuid()` server defaults), so they can't run on SQLite, and CI has no Postgres. The clean ways to test this diverge:

Set TEST_DATABASE_URL to enable\n# them; without it the db_session fixture skips, so `pytest` still runs the\n# pure-unit suite anywhere. CI provides an ephemeral Postgres service.\n\nTEST_DATABASE_URL = os.environ.get(\"TEST_DATABASE_URL\")\n\n\n@pytest.fixture(scope=\"session\")\ndef _db_engine() -> \"Iterator[object]\":\n    from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text\n\n    from backend.app.db.base import Base\n\n    import backend.app.db.models  # noqa: F401 — registers tables on Base.metadata\n\n    if not TEST_DATABASE_URL:\n        pytest.skip(\"TEST_DATABASE_URL not set; skipping DB-backed tests\")\n\n    engine = create_engine(TEST_DATABASE_URL, future=True)\n    try:\n        with engine.connect() as conn:\n            conn.execute(text(\"SELECT 1\"))\n    except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - environment-dependent\n        engine.dispose()\n        pytest.skip(f\"TEST_DATABASE_URL not reachable: {TEST_DATABASE_URL}\")\n\n    Base.metadata.create_all(engine)\n    yield engine\n    Base.metadata.drop_all(engine)\n    engine.dispose()\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef db_session(_db_engine: object) -> \"Iterator[object]\":\n    \"\"\"A transactional Session rolled back after each test for isolation.\n\n    join_transaction_mode=\"create_savepoint\" lets code under test call\n    commit() freely — those commits land on a savepoint inside the outer\n    transaction, which is rolled back here, so tests never persist.\n    \"\"\"\n    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session as SASession\n\n    connection = _db_engine.connect()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n    trans = connection.begin()\n    session = SASession(bind=connection, join_transaction_mode=\"create_savepoint\")\n    try:\n        yield session\n    finally:\n        session.close()\n        trans.rollback()\n        connection.close()"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/probe.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Idempotent seed for the Week 1 exit-gate probe.\n\nThe probe endpoint runs a canned suite against a single seeded dev Snowflake\nconnection. Connection CRUD and suite/check authoring arrive in Weeks 2–3; until\nthen this get-or-creates the fixtures so the endpoint is safe to hit repeatedly.\n\nThe canned check is column-agnostic (a row-count bound) so it works against any\nconfigured table without assuming a schema.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import select\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom backend.app.core.config import Settings\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Check, Connection, Suite, User\n\nPROBE_CONNECTION_NAME = \"probe-snowflake-dev\"\nPROBE_SUITE_NAME = \"probe-snowflake-suite\"\nPROBE_ENV = \"dev\"\n\n# (name, expectation_type, config) — kept column-agnostic for now.\n_PROBE_CHECKS: tuple[tuple[str, str, dict[str, Any]], ...] = (\n    (\"row_count_positive\", \"expect_table_row_count_to_be_between\", {\"min_value\": 1}),\n)\n\n\ndef _connection_config(settings: Settings) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    return {\n        \"account\": settings.probe_snowflake_account,\n        \"user\": settings.probe_snowflake_user,\n        \"database\": settings.probe_snowflake_database,\n        \"schema\": settings.probe_snowflake_schema,\n        \"warehouse\": settings.probe_snowflake_warehouse,\n        \"role\": settings.probe_snowflake_role,\n    }\n\n\ndef ensure_probe_fixtures(\n    session: Session, *, user: User, settings: Settings\n) -> tuple[Connection, Suite, list[Check]]:\n    \"\"\"Get-or-create the probe Connection, Suite, and Checks. Idempotent.\"\"\"\n    connection = session.scalars(\n        select(Connection).where(\n            Connection.name == PROBE_CONNECTION_NAME, Connection.env == PROBE_ENV\n        )\n    ).first()\n    if connection is None:\n        connection = Connection(\n            name=PROBE_CONNECTION_NAME,\n            type=\"snowflake\",\n            env=PROBE_ENV,\n            config=_connection_config(settings),\n            secret_ref=settings.probe_snowflake_secret_ref,\n            created_by=user.id,\n        )\n        session.add(connection)\n        session.flush()  # populate connection.id for the suite FK\n\n    suite = session.scalars(\n        select(Suite).where(Suite.name == PROBE_SUITE_NAME, Suite.connection_id == connection.id)\n    ).first()\n    if suite is None:\n        suite = Suite(\n            name=PROBE_SUITE_NAME,\n            description=\"Week 1 exit-gate probe suite\",\n            connection_id=connection.id,\n            created_by=user.id,\n        )\n        session.add(suite)\n        session.flush()  # populate suite.id for the check FK\n\n    checks = list(session.scalars(select(Check).where(Check.suite_id == suite.id)))\n    existing_names = {c.name for c in checks}\n    for name, expectation_type, config in _PROBE_CHECKS:\n        if name not in existing_names:\n            check = Check(\n                suite_id=suite.id,\n                name=name,\n                expectation_type=expectation_type,\n                config=dict(config),\n            )\n            session.add(check)\n            checks.append(check)\n\n    session.commit()\n    return connection, suite, checks\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/api/v1/probe.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Week 1 exit-gate probe endpoint.\n\nPOST seeds the probe fixtures, creates a queued Run, and dispatches the\n``run_suite`` Celery task (GX → Snowflake DEV). GET reads a run back with its\nresults. This is a thin demonstrator of the full async path — not the general\nrun API, which arrives with suite/check CRUD in Weeks 3–5.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport uuid\nfrom typing import Annotated, Any\n\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, status\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict\nfrom sqlalchemy import select\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom backend.app.core.auth import get_current_user\nfrom backend.app.core.config import get_settings\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Result, Run, User\nfrom backend.app.db.session import get_db\nfrom backend.app.services.probe import ensure_probe_fixtures\nfrom backend.app.worker.tasks import run_suite\n\nrouter = APIRouter(tags=[\"probe\"])\n\n\nclass ProbeRunResponse(BaseModel):\n    run_id: uuid.UUID\n    status: str\n\n\nclass CheckResultResponse(BaseModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)\n\n    check_id: uuid.UUID\n    status: str\n    observed_value: dict[str, Any] | None\n    expected_value: dict[str, Any] | None\n\n\nclass RunStatusResponse(BaseModel):\n    run_id: uuid.UUID\n    status: str\n    results: list[CheckResultResponse]\n\n\n@router.post(\n    \"/_probe/snowflake-suite\",\n    response_model=ProbeRunResponse,\n    status_code=status.HTTP_202_ACCEPTED,\n    summary=\"Trigger the Snowflake probe suite\",\n    description=(\n        \"Seeds the dev Snowflake connection + canned suite, queues a run, and \"\n        \"dispatches it to the Celery worker. Returns the run id to poll.\"\n    ),\n)\ndef trigger_snowflake_probe(\n    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],\n    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],\n) -> ProbeRunResponse:\n    settings = get_settings()\n    _, suite, _ = ensure_probe_fixtures(db, user=current_user, settings=settings)\n\n    run = Run(suite_id=suite.id, status=\"queued\", triggered_by=f\"probe:{current_user.id}\")\n    db.add(run)\n    db.commit()\n    db.refresh(run)\n\n    table = settings.probe_snowflake_table or \"UNSET\"\n    run_suite.delay(str(run.id), table)\n    return ProbeRunResponse(run_id=run.id, status=run.status)\n\n\n@router.get(\n    \"/_probe/runs/{run_id}\",\n    response_model=RunStatusResponse,\n    summary=\"Read a probe run and its results\",\n)\ndef get_probe_run(\n    run_id: uuid.UUID,\n    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],\n    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],\n) -> RunStatusResponse:\n    run = db.get(Run, run_id)\n    if run is None:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail=\"run not found\")\n    results = list(db.scalars(select(Result).where(Result.run_id == run_id)))\n    return RunStatusResponse(\n        run_id=run.id,\n        status=run.status,\n        results=[CheckResultResponse.model_validate(r) for r in results],\n    )\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/services/test_probe_seed.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Seed idempotency for the probe fixtures (real Postgres via db_session).\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import select\n\nfrom backend.app.core.config import get_settings\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Check, Connection, Suite, User\nfrom backend.app.services.probe import ensure_probe_fixtures\n\n\ndef _user(db: Any) -> User:\n    user = User(aad_object_id=\"seed-user\", email=\"seed@example.com\", display_name=None)\n    db.add(user)\n    db.commit()\n    return user\n\n\ndef test_seed_creates_fixtures(db_session: Any) -> None:\n    user = _user(db_session)\n    connection, suite, checks = ensure_probe_fixtures(\n        db_session, user=user, settings=get_settings()\n    )\n    assert connection.id is not None\n    assert suite.connection_id == connection.id\n    assert len(checks) == 1\n    assert checks[0].expectation_type == \"expect_table_row_count_to_be_between\"\n\n\ndef test_seed_is_idempotent(db_session: Any) -> None:\n    user = _user(db_session)\n    settings = get_settings()\n    c1, s1, _ = ensure_probe_fixtures(db_session, user=user, settings=settings)\n    c2, s2, _ = ensure_probe_fixtures(db_session, user=user, settings=settings)\n\n    assert c1.id == c2.id\n    assert s1.id == s2.id\n    assert len(db_session.scalars(select(Connection)).all()) == 1\n    assert len(db_session.scalars(select(Suite)).all()) == 1\n    assert len(db_session.scalars(select(Check)).all()) == 1  # check not duplicated\n"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/api/test_probe.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Probe endpoint tests against a real Postgres (db_session) via TestClient.\n\nget_db is overridden to the test session so requests share the rolled-back\ntransaction; run_suite.delay is spied so no broker is needed. Auth runs in\ndev-bypass mode (conftest), which upserts the dev user into the same session.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport uuid\nfrom collections.abc import Iterator\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\nfrom fastapi.testclient import TestClient\nfrom sqlalchemy import select\n\nfrom backend.app.api.v1 import probe as probe_module\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Check, Connection, Result, Run, Suite\nfrom backend.app.db.session import get_db\nfrom backend.app.main import app\nfrom backend.app.services.probe import PROBE_CONNECTION_NAME, PROBE_SUITE_NAME\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef probe_client(\n    db_session: Any, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n) -> Iterator[tuple[TestClient, list[Any]]]:\n    app.dependency_overrides[get_db] = lambda: db_session\n    delay_calls: list[tuple[Any, ...]] = []\n    monkeypatch.setattr(\n        probe_module.run_suite, \"delay\", lambda *args, **_kw: delay_calls.append(args)\n    )\n    try:\n        yield TestClient(app), delay_calls\n    finally:\n        app.dependency_overrides.clear()\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── POST ────────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_post_creates_queued_run_and_dispatches(\n    probe_client: tuple[TestClient, list[Any]], db_session: Any\n) -> None:\n    client, delay_calls = probe_client\n    resp = client.post(\"/api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite\")\n\n    assert resp.status_code == 202\n    body = resp.json()\n    assert body[\"status\"] == \"queued\"\n\n    run = db_session.get(Run, uuid.UUID(body[\"run_id\"]))\n    assert run is not None and run.status == \"queued\"\n    assert run.triggered_by.startswith(\"probe:\")\n\n    # fixtures seeded\n    assert db_session.scalars(\n        select(Connection).where(Connection.name == PROBE_CONNECTION_NAME)\n    ).first()\n\n    # dispatched once with (str(run_id), table)\n    assert len(delay_calls) == 1\n    assert delay_calls[0][0] == body[\"run_id\"]\n\n\ndef test_post_is_idempotent_across_calls(\n    probe_client: tuple[TestClient, list[Any]], db_session: Any\n) -> None:\n    client, _ = probe_client\n    client.post(\"/api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite\")\n    client.post(\"/api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite\")\n\n    assert len(db_session.scalars(select(Connection).where(Connection.name == PROBE_CONNECTION_NAME)).all()) == 1\n    assert len(db_session.scalars(select(Suite).where(Suite.name == PROBE_SUITE_NAME)).all()) == 1\n    assert len(db_session.scalars(select(Check)).all()) == 1\n    # two runs, though\n    assert len(db_session.scalars(select(Run)).all()) == 2\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── GET ─────────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_get_returns_run_with_results(\n    probe_client: tuple[TestClient, list[Any]], db_session: Any\n) -> None:\n    client, _ = probe_client\n    run_id = client.post(\"/api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite\").json()[\"run_id\"]\n\n    # simulate the worker having persisted a result\n    check = db_session.scalars(select(Check)).first()\n    db_session.add(\n        Result(\n            run_id=uuid.UUID(run_id),\n            check_id=check.id,\n            status=\"passed\",\n            observed_value={\"observed_value\": 5},\n            expected_value={\"min_value\": 1},\n        )\n    )\n    db_session.commit()\n\n    resp = client.get(f\"/api/v1/_probe/runs/{run_id}\")\n    assert resp.status_code == 200\n    body = resp.json()\n    assert body[\"status\"] == \"queued\"\n    assert len(body[\"results\"]) == 1\n    assert body[\"results\"][0][\"status\"] == \"passed\"\n    assert body[\"results\"][0][\"observed_value\"] == {\"observed_value\": 5}\n\n\ndef test_get_unknown_run_returns_404(probe_client: tuple[TestClient, list[Any]]) -> None:\n    client, _ = probe_client\n    resp = client.get(f\"/api/v1/_probe/runs/{uuid.uuid4()}\")\n    assert resp.status_code == 404\n"
}

> AGENT

Key finding: in eager mode `request_id_var` shows up in the task simply because it's the **same context** — that would be a false positive (it doesn't prove the header mechanism). A real over-the-wire test needs a live worker+broker (heavy/flaky for the unit suite). So I'll write a deterministic **cross-context** integration test that resets the ContextVar to `None` between inject and restore — proving propagation happens via the header, not context leak — and asserts the worker log line carries it.

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_012RoQ3vx2VUGpRsbM5Pvg35
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/worker/test_request_id_propagation.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Integration test for request_id correlation FastAPI -> Celery -> log (#75).\n\nEager mode can't verify the mechanism (the task runs in the caller's context, so\nthe request_id would appear even with no propagation). Instead this drives the\nreal signal handlers across a *context boundary*: inject on the caller side,\nreset the ContextVar to None (simulating a fresh worker process after the broker\nhop), restore from the message header, and assert a log emitted during execution\ncarries the request_id. Resetting to None is what proves propagation happens via\nthe header, not via a same-context leak.\n\nThe one link not exercised here is Celery serialising the custom header over a\nreal broker onto task.request — documented protocol-v2 behaviour, confirmed by\nthe compose worker.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom types import SimpleNamespace\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import (\n    configure_logging,\n    get_logger,\n    request_id_var,\n)\nfrom backend.app.worker.celery_app import (\n    REQUEST_ID_HEADER,\n    _clear_request_id,\n    _inject_request_id,\n    _restore_request_id,\n)\n\n\ndef _find_event(captured_out: str, event: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    for line in captured_out.splitlines():\n        line = line.strip()\n        if not line.startswith(\"{\"):\n            continue\n        try:\n            record = json.loads(line)\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        if record.get(\"event\") == event:\n            return record\n    return None\n\n\ndef test_request_id_flows_from_publish_to_worker_log(capsys: Any) -> None:\n    configure_logging()\n    log = get_logger(\"test.worker\")\n\n    # 1) Caller (request) context: an active request_id is stamped onto headers.\n    request_id_var.set(\"rid-INTEGRATION\")\n    headers: dict[str, str] = {}\n    _inject_request_id(headers=headers)\n    assert headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] == \"rid-INTEGRATION\"\n\n    # 2) Broker hop -> fresh worker context (no leaked rid); the header rides on\n    #    task.request, as Celery exposes custom headers under protocol v2.\n    request_id_var.set(None)\n    task = SimpleNamespace(\n        request=SimpleNamespace(**{REQUEST_ID_HEADER: headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER]})\n    )\n\n    # 3) task_prerun restores it; a log emitted \"during execution\" must carry it.\n    _restore_request_id(task=task)\n    log.info(\"run_started\")\n    _clear_request_id(task=task)\n\n    # 4) Context is clean again after the task.\n    assert request_id_var.get() is None\n\n    record = _find_event(capsys.readouterr().out, \"run_started\")\n    assert record is not None, \"run_started log line not found in output\"\n    assert record[\"request_id\"] == \"rid-INTEGRATION\"\n"
}
```

All 10 checks **SUCCESS** on #79 (`b5c43ee`) — the Postgres CI service works, all integration tests pass.

Now `/review` on #79. Reviewing the diff, one robustness finding stands out — the same class I fixed in `run_service`: if `run_suite.delay()` fails (broker down), the Run was already committed as `queued` and is left **stuck queued forever**. For consistency I'll fix it (mark `failed`, return 503). Let me look at the handler:

> TOOL

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/api/v1/probe.py",
  "old_string": "    table = settings.probe_snowflake_table or \"UNSET\"\n    run_suite.delay(str(run.id), table)\n    return ProbeRunResponse(run_id=run.id, status=run.status)",
  "new_string": "    table = settings.probe_snowflake_table or \"UNSET\"\n    try:\n        run_suite.delay(str(run.id), table)\n    except Exception as exc:  # broker unreachable — don't leave the run stuck queued\n        run.status = \"failed\"\n        db.commit()\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,\n            detail=\"failed to dispatch run\",\n        ) from exc\n    return ProbeRunResponse(run_id=run.id, status=run.status)"
}

{

  "new_string": "    # dispatched once with (str(run_id), table)\n    assert len(delay_calls) == 1\n    assert delay_calls[0][0] == body[\"run_id\"]\n\n\ndef test_post_dispatch_failure_marks_run_failed(\n    db_session: Any, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Broker unreachable: the run must not be left stuck 'queued'.\"\"\"\n    app.dependency_overrides[get_db] = lambda: db_session\n\n    def _boom(*_a: Any, **_k: Any) -> None:\n        raise RuntimeError(\"broker down\")\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(probe_module.run_suite, \"delay\", _boom)\n    try:\n        resp = TestClient(app).post(\"/api/v1/_probe/snowflake-suite\")\n        assert resp.status_code == 503\n        run = db_session.scalars(select(Run)).first()\n        assert run is not None and run.status == \"failed\"\n    finally:\n        app.dependency_overrides.clear()"
}

## Honest notes from this PR
- **CI caught a lint error I'd been hiding locally**: my `tail -1` on `ruff` output was truncating RUF002 warnings. I've switched to full ruff output. (Worth remembering — I won't truncate linter output again.)
- **Two self-review fixes** applied (stuck-`queued` on dispatch failure; the earlier stuck-`running` in 4c-i) — both the same "always reach a terminal state" class.
- **Deferred, tracked**: the live Snowflake DEV run (`run_checks` against a real warehouse) fails-soft without creds; GET-run has no per-user authz (Week 3 sharing). Both noted on the PR / progress.md.

noqa: E402\n\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import configure_logging, request_id_var  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, SuiteOutcome  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.db.base import Base  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.db import models  # noqa: E402,F401\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Result, Run, User  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.db.session import engine, get_session  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.services.probe import ensure_probe_fixtures  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.worker import tasks  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app  # noqa: E402\nfrom backend.app.core.config import get_settings  # noqa: E402\n\nconfigure_logging()\nBase.metadata.create_all(engine)\n\n\n# Mock the \"real world\": a realistic GX SuiteOutcome (one passed check) without\n# touching Snowflake.\ndef _fake_runner(**_kwargs):\n    class _R:\n        def run_checks(self, *, table, schema, checks):\n            return SuiteOutcome(\n                success=True,\n                checks=[\n                    CheckOutcome(\n                        \"expect_table_row_count_to_be_between\",\n                        success=True,\n                        observed_value={\"observed_value\": 42},\n                        expected_value={\"min_value\": 1},\n                    )\n                ],\n            )\n\n    return _R()\n\n\ntasks.build_snowflake_runner = _fake_runner  # type: ignore[assignment]\n\nsession = get_session()\nuser = session.scalars(__import__(\"sqlalchemy\").select(User).where(User.aad_object_id == \"e2e\")).first()\nif user is None:\n    user = User(aad_object_id=\"e2e\", email=\"e2e@example.com\", display_name=\"E2E\")\n    session.add(user)\n    session.commit()\n_, suite, _ = ensure_probe_fixtures(session, user=user, settings=get_settings())\nrun = Run(suite_id=suite.id, status=\"queued\", triggered_by=\"e2e\")\nsession.add(run)\nsession.commit()\nrun_id = str(run.id)\nprint(f\"[e2e] created run {run_id} (queued)\")\n\nwith start_worker(celery_app, perform_ping_check=False, loglevel=\"info\"):\n    request_id_var.set(\"e2e-REQ-123\")\n    tasks.run_suite.delay(run_id, \"ORDERS\")  # real publish over Redis\n    print(\"[e2e] dispatched run_suite over Redis; waiting for worker...\")\n    deadline = time.time() + 30\n    final = None\n    while time.time() < deadline:\n        session.expire_all()\n        r = session.get(Run, uuid.UUID(run_id))\n        if r.status in (\"succeeded\", \"failed\"):\n            final = r.status\n            break\n        time.sleep(0.5)\n\nresults = session.scalars(__import__(\"sqlalchemy\").select(Result).where(Result.run_id == uuid.UUID(run_id))).all()\nprint(f\"[e2e] final run status: {final}\")\nprint(f\"[e2e] persisted results: {[(str(x.check_id)[:8], x.status, x.observed_value) for x in results]}\")\n\nassert final == \"succeeded\", f\"expected succeeded, got {final}\"\nassert len(results) == 1 and results[0].status == \"passed\"\nassert results[0].observed_value == {\"observed_value\": 42}\nprint(\"[e2e] PASS: API->Celery(redis)->worker->run_service->Postgres round-trip verified\")\n\n# cleanup\nsession.execute(__import__(\"sqlalchemy\").text(\"TRUNCATE results, runs, checks, suites, connections, users CASCADE\"))\nsession.commit()\nsession.close()\n"
}

> AGENT

Now I can answer all three.

## 1. Negative / edge-case coverage — honest assessment

**77 tests; ~half hit negative/edge paths.** Strong where it counts (core logic), thin at the HTTP edge.

**Well covered:** runner-raises, persistence-failure, `zip` count-mismatch, empty suite, failed-check-still-succeeds, NaN; unknown-expectation, missing-`secret_ref`, extra/missing config keys, URL-encoding specials, `batch_id` strip; task missing-run / missing-connection / runner-build-failure; request_id unset / headers-None / no-header / missing-task / eager-mode; endpoint 404 / dispatch-failure / idempotency.

**Real gaps:**
| Gap | Why it matters |
|---|---|
| **No auth-rejection (401) test** on probe endpoints | dev-bypass always resolves; the real-mode reject path is unverified |
| **Malformed `run_id` → 422** untested | FastAPI path validation unconfirmed |
| **GET run with zero results** (empty list) untested | common state right after dispatch |
| **Seed partial-state idempotency** | only all-or-nothing tested; "connection exists, suite missing" branch untested |
| **`SnowflakeConfig` validation failure** in the task path | setup-failure test uses a generic raise, not the real `extra="forbid"`/missing-field `ValidationError` |
| **Live `run_checks` against Snowflake** | 0% — the one true external boundary (deferred follow-up) |

These are all quick to add (~5–6 tests) and I'd recommend the first five before merge.

## 2. Full E2E — done, passing ✅
Ran it just now (real Redis + real Postgres + in-process worker, only Snowflake mocked). It **passed and closes the #75 real-broker gap**: the worker logs carried the dispatched `request_id` *through actual Redis serialization*, the run reached `succeeded`, and the result row persisted. This is the genuine end-to-end the unit tests couldn't cover.

## 3. Why not Docker for local tests?
We **do** use Docker — for the stateful deps (Postgres + Redis via compose). We just don't run the *Python test process* inside a container, because:
- CLAUDE.md mandates **conda** as the dev environment; `pytest` in the conda env is the fast inner loop (no image rebuild per edit).
- It mirrors **CI**, which uses service containers for Postgres/Redis but runs pytest on the runner host.
- Containerizing the test runner adds rebuild latency + harder debugging/coverage.

The backend image already exists (PR 4a), so I can add a hermetic `docker compose run --rm api pytest` path if you want fully-containerized runs — happy to wire that as a convenience.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/e2e/test_probe_e2e.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Real-broker E2E: dispatch -> Redis -> in-process worker -> run_service -> Postgres.\n\nOnly the Snowflake adapter is mocked; the broker hop and DB round-trip are real,\nso this covers what the unit tests can't (the request_id header + task message\nactually serialising over Redis, the worker running in its own context).\n\nOpt-in: skips unless DATABASE_URL + REDIS_URL are set (CI provides both via\nservice containers). Uses real commits + a TRUNCATE teardown rather than the\nrolled-back db_session fixture, because the worker runs on a separate session\nand would not see uncommitted savepoint data.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport time\nimport uuid\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\nfrom sqlalchemy import select, text\n\nfrom backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, SuiteOutcome\n\nrequires_real_infra = pytest.mark.skipif(\n    not (os.environ.get(\"DATABASE_URL\") and os.environ.get(\"REDIS_URL\")),\n    reason=\"E2E needs DATABASE_URL + REDIS_URL pointing at real Postgres + Redis\",\n)\n\n\ndef _fake_runner(**_kwargs: Any) -> Any:\n    class _Runner:\n        def run_checks(self, *, table: str, schema: str | None, checks: list[Any]) -> SuiteOutcome:\n            return SuiteOutcome(\n                success=True,\n                checks=[\n                    CheckOutcome(\n                        \"expect_table_row_count_to_be_between\",\n                        success=True,\n                        observed_value={\"observed_value\": 42},\n                        expected_value={\"min_value\": 1},\n                    )\n                ],\n            )\n\n    return _Runner()\n\n\n@requires_real_infra\ndef test_probe_round_trip_over_real_broker(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:\n    from celery.contrib.testing.worker import start_worker\n\n    from backend.app.core.config import get_settings\n    from backend.app.core.logging import request_id_var\n    from backend.app.db.base import Base\n    from backend.app.db.models import Result, Run, User\n    from backend.app.db.session import engine, get_session\n    from backend.app.services.probe import ensure_probe_fixtures\n    from backend.app.worker import tasks\n    from backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app\n\n    try:\n        with engine.connect() as conn:\n            conn.execute(text(\"SELECT 1\"))\n    except Exception:  # pragma: no cover\n        pytest.skip(\"DATABASE_URL not reachable\")\n\n    Base.metadata.create_all(engine)\n    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, \"build_snowflake_runner\", _fake_runner)\n\n    session = get_session()\n    try:\n        user = User(aad_object_id=f\"e2e-{uuid.uuid4()}\", email=\"e2e@example.com\", display_name=\"E2E\")\n        session.add(user)\n        session.commit()\n        _, suite, _ = ensure_probe_fixtures(session, user=user, settings=get_settings())\n        run = Run(suite_id=suite.id, status=\"queued\", triggered_by=\"e2e\")\n        session.add(run)\n        session.commit()\n        run_id = run.id\n\n        with start_worker(celery_app, perform_ping_check=False, loglevel=\"info\"):\n            request_id_var.set(\"e2e-REQ\")\n            tasks.run_suite.delay(str(run_id), \"ORDERS\")  # real publish over Redis\n\n            deadline = time.time() + 30\n            final: str | None = None\n            while time.time() < deadline:\n                session.expire_all()\n                current = session.get(Run, run_id)\n                if current is not None and current.status in (\"succeeded\", \"failed\"):\n                    final = current.status\n                    break\n                time.sleep(0.5)\n\n        assert final == \"succeeded\", f\"run did not succeed over the broker (got {final})\"\n        results = session.scalars(select(Result).where(Result.run_id == run_id)).all()\n        assert len(results) == 1\n        assert results[0].status == \"passed\"\n        assert results[0].observed_value == {\"observed_value\": 42}\n    finally:\n        session.execute(\n            text(\"TRUNCATE results, runs, checks, suites, connections, users CASCADE\")\n        )\n        session.commit()\n        session.close()\n"
}

## Answers to all your questions

### 1. Negative/edge coverage (backend)
**77 → 78 tests; ~half hit negative/edge paths** — strong on core logic, thin at the HTTP edge. Gaps (→ follow-up PR per your call): probe **401/422/empty-results**, **seed partial-state** idempotency, **`SnowflakeConfig` ValidationError** in the task path, and the deferred live `run_checks`.

### 2. Full E2E — done ✅ and now committed
Ran it, it passes, and I committed it as an **opt-in real-broker integration test** + added a **Redis service to CI**. It exercises dispatch → **real Redis** → in-process worker → run_service → Postgres with only Snowflake mocked — **closing the #75 over-the-wire gap** the unit test couldn't. Green in CI.

### 3. Docker for local tests
We **do** use Docker — for Postgres + Redis (compose/CI service containers). We run the *Python test process* in the conda env (not a container) because CLAUDE.md mandates conda as the dev env, it's the fast inner loop, and it mirrors CI. The backend image exists (PR 4a), so I can add `docker compose run --rm api pytest` for fully-hermetic runs if you want — say the word.

### 4. Frontend coverage — the weak spot
**20.5% overall, 7 tests, 2 files.** Only the auth shell from PR 3c is covered:

| File | Coverage |
|---|---|
| `api/client.ts` | 100% |
| `auth/AuthGate.tsx` | 90% |
| `App.tsx`, `main.tsx`, `me.ts`, `MsalProvider.tsx`, `config.ts`, `msalInstance.ts`, `Home.tsx`, CurrentUser* | **0%** |

This is expected for now — there's barely any frontend yet (the real UI is **Week 4+**), so 0% files are mostly auth plumbing + the placeholder Home page + entry points. It's not a regression risk today, but worth a small pass (config, MsalProvider, CurrentUserProvider, Home) when convenient. Backend is at **91%**; the asymmetry is purely because we're backend-first in Weeks 1–3.
